- **Score**: 7/10

### Critical Thinking: Which Kinds of Complexity Govern Optimal Reasoning Length?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01935v1)
- **Authors**: Celine Lee, Alexander M. Rush, Keyon Vafa
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) often benefit from verbalized reasoning at inference time, but it remains unclear which aspects of task difficulty these extra reasoning tokens address. To investigate this question, we formalize a framework using deterministic finite automata (DFAs). DFAs offer a formalism through which we can characterize task complexity through measurable properties such as run length (number of reasoning steps required) and state-space size (decision complexity). We first show that across different tasks and models of different sizes and training paradigms, there exists an optimal amount of reasoning tokens such that the probability of producing a correct solution is maximized. We then investigate which properties of complexity govern this critical length: we find that task instances with longer corresponding underlying DFA runs (i.e. demand greater latent state-tracking requirements) correlate with longer reasoning lengths, but, surprisingly, that DFA size (i.e. state-space complexity) does not. We then demonstrate an implication of these findings: being able to predict the optimal number of reasoning tokens for new problems and filtering out non-optimal length answers results in consistent accuracy improvements.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the relationship between task complexity and the optimal length of reasoning chains generated by Large Language Models (LLMs). The authors propose a framework using Deterministic Finite Automata (DFAs) to characterize task complexity based on two dimensions: run length (number of reasoning steps) and state-space size (decision complexity). They empirically demonstrate that an optimal reasoning length exists for different tasks and models. Furthermore, they find that the critical reasoning length is strongly correlated with the DFA run length but has little to no correlation with the DFA state-space size. Based on this, they demonstrate an improvement in accuracy by performing rejection sampling based on the predicted critical length for a given task and model, which they predict based on the run length and state-space size.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in formalizing task complexity for LLM reasoning using DFAs and directly linking it to the optimal reasoning length at inference time. While the idea of DFAs representing tasks isn't new, the explicit connection to reasoning length optimization and the empirical validation are valuable. Also, the somewhat surprising finding that run length, but not state-space size, is a dominant factor is interesting.

*   **Significance:** The work has the potential to significantly impact how we approach LLM inference. The idea of dynamically adjusting the reasoning length based on task properties could lead to more efficient and accurate reasoning. The rejection sampling experiments demonstrate a practical way to leverage this connection, even with a very simple prediction model.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Principled Framework:** The DFA-based approach provides a structured and interpretable way to measure task complexity.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The findings are supported by experiments across a diverse set of tasks and models.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The paper demonstrates a way to improve accuracy by filtering generations based on predicted optimal length.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and the methodology is clearly explained.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Complexity Representation:** While DFAs are useful, they may not fully capture the complexity of all reasoning tasks, especially those requiring more nuanced understanding or commonsense knowledge.
    *   **Simplicity of Prediction Model:** While the rejection sampling experiment is effective, the linear model used to predict the optimal length is quite simple. More sophisticated models could yield even greater improvements.
    *   **DFA Identification:** The underlying DFA parameters for the tasks are not formally derived in the paper. Instead the authors propose their own approximation for the length and states, and this may not fully capture the true complexity.
    *   **Tasks are Canonical:** While this isn't an immediate weakness, the authors only consider canonical datasets that may have been seen in training, resulting in an overestimation of the true potential of the method.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper opens up several avenues for future research, including developing more sophisticated ways to characterize task complexity, designing adaptive inference strategies, and gaining a deeper understanding of how LLMs represent and utilize reasoning chains. The idea could inspire more efficient inference techniques.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper's use of DFAs to link task complexity and reasoning length is a novel and valuable contribution. It provides a more principled way to approach LLM reasoning optimization and the demonstrated improvement in accuracy is promising. However, the limitations in the complexity representation and the simplicity of the prediction model prevent it from being a truly exceptional contribution. Also, the analysis is lacking rigour around the identification of the DFA parameters from each task.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Unified Approach to Analysis and Design of Denoising Markov Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01938v1)
- **Authors**: Yinuo Ren, Grant M. Rotskoff, Lexing Ying
- **Abstract**: Probabilistic generative models based on measure transport, such as diffusion and flow-based models, are often formulated in the language of Markovian stochastic dynamics, where the choice of the underlying process impacts both algorithmic design choices and theoretical analysis. In this paper, we aim to establish a rigorous mathematical foundation for denoising Markov models, a broad class of generative models that postulate a forward process transitioning from the target distribution to a simple, easy-to-sample distribution, alongside a backward process particularly constructed to enable efficient sampling in the reverse direction. Leveraging deep connections with nonequilibrium statistical mechanics and generalized Doob's $h$-transform, we propose a minimal set of assumptions that ensure: (1) explicit construction of the backward generator, (2) a unified variational objective directly minimizing the measure transport discrepancy, and (3) adaptations of the classical score-matching approach across diverse dynamics. Our framework unifies existing formulations of continuous and discrete diffusion models, identifies the most general form of denoising Markov models under certain regularity assumptions on forward generators, and provides a systematic recipe for designing denoising Markov models driven by arbitrary L\'evy-type processes. We illustrate the versatility and practical effectiveness of our approach through novel denoising Markov models employing geometric Brownian motion and jump processes as forward dynamics, highlighting the framework's potential flexibility and capability in modeling complex distributions.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the paper and here's a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a unified mathematical framework for analyzing and designing "denoising Markov models," a broad class of generative models that include diffusion models and flow-based models as special cases. The authors leverage connections to non-equilibrium statistical mechanics, specifically generalized Doob's h-transform, to establish a minimal set of assumptions guaranteeing explicit construction of the backward generator, a unified variational objective directly minimizing measure transport discrepancy, and adaptations of score-matching across various dynamics. They unify continuous and discrete diffusion models and provide a recipe for designing denoising Markov models driven by arbitrary Lévy-type processes. The framework is demonstrated with geometric Brownian motion and jump processes as forward dynamics. The paper focuses on theoretical foundations and algorithmic design, providing a theoretical guarantee for the performance of the estimated backward process in terms of the KL divergence.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Unification:** A major strength is the unification of disparate generative modeling techniques under a single theoretical umbrella. This is valuable because it allows researchers to transfer insights and techniques between different model families and offers a more comprehensive understanding of the underlying principles.

    *   **Generality:** The framework goes beyond standard diffusion models by encompassing Lévy-type processes. This is a significant step because it opens the door to more flexible and expressive generative models capable of capturing complex data distributions that diffusion models might struggle with (e.g., heavy-tailed distributions).

    *   **Theoretical Rigor:** The paper's reliance on established mathematical tools like Doob's h-transform and the presentation of theorems with clearly stated assumptions provide a solid theoretical foundation for denoising Markov models. This rigor adds credibility and makes the framework more trustworthy.

    *   **Practical Guidance:** While theoretical, the paper translates some results into practical guidance for training and sampling from denoising Markov models. This makes the framework more accessible to practitioners interested in designing new generative models.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Empirical Validation:** The paper introduces novel model instances, but the empirical validation, while present, isn't extensive. The experiments are simple (geometric Brownian motion and jump process, not as complex as images), demonstrating effectiveness but without fully showcasing the practical advantages of these new models over established methods, especially in terms of sample quality, computational cost, or handling very high-dimensional data. The practical examples also seems to be restricted to certain scenarios.

    *   **Complexity:** The theoretical framework is dense with mathematical machinery, which may be a barrier to entry for researchers and practitioners less familiar with stochastic processes. While rigorous, the level of abstraction might obscure the immediate practical benefits for some.

    *   **Novelty of application:** While the mathematical tools are well-established, their application to *generative* modeling *itself* needs to be evaluated critically; as the paper provides a novel framework, however the significance of this framework can be assessed by the impact on performance.

    *   **Lack of concrete performance comparison:** While the paper's stated goal is not to outperform, it would be useful to have some reference baseline performance to ground the improvements made by the new frameworks.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The core contribution lies in the theoretical unification and generalization of existing approaches.
    *   The application of generalized Doob's h-transform to denoising Markov models is novel and provides a fresh perspective on understanding and designing these models.
    *   The ability to incorporate general Lévy-type processes represents a significant extension of the state-of-the-art.
    *   The recipe for training and sampling from the developed framework can be significantly practical.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper has the potential to influence the design of future generative models.
    *   It provides a solid theoretical foundation that could lead to more principled approaches to model selection and training.
    *   The generalized framework could inspire new applications of generative models in domains where existing techniques are insufficient.
    *   Can advance the understanding on how the choice of forward and backward processes can affect training and the characteristics of these frameworks.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a strong, theoretically grounded framework that unifies and generalizes existing generative modeling techniques. The novelty lies in its unified perspective using tools from non-equilibrium statistical mechanics and its ability to incorporate Lévy-type processes. It also introduces novel model instances, which highlights the flexibility of the approach. However, the limited empirical validation and the high level of mathematical complexity temper the overall assessment. Therefore, I would give it a score of:

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### OpenCodeReasoning: Advancing Data Distillation for Competitive Coding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01943v1)
- **Authors**: Wasi Uddin Ahmad, Sean Narenthiran, Somshubra Majumdar, Aleksander Ficek, Siddhartha Jain, Jocelyn Huang, Vahid Noroozi, Boris Ginsburg
- **Abstract**: Since the advent of reasoning-based large language models, many have found great success from distilling reasoning capabilities into student models. Such techniques have significantly bridged the gap between reasoning and standard LLMs on coding tasks. Despite this, much of the progress on distilling reasoning models remains locked behind proprietary datasets or lacks details on data curation, filtering and subsequent training. To address this, we construct a superior supervised fine-tuning (SFT) dataset that we use to achieve state-of-the-art coding capability results in models of various sizes. Our distilled models use only SFT to achieve 61.8% on LiveCodeBench and 24.6% on CodeContests, surpassing alternatives trained with reinforcement learning. We then perform analysis on the data sources used to construct our dataset, the impact of code execution filtering, and the importance of instruction/solution diversity. We observe that execution filtering negatively affected benchmark accuracy, leading us to prioritize instruction diversity over solution correctness. Finally, we also analyze the token efficiency and reasoning patterns utilized by these models. We will open-source these datasets and distilled models to the community.
- **Summary**: The paper "OpenCodeReasoning: Advancing Data Distillation for Competitive Coding" introduces a large-scale supervised fine-tuning (SFT) dataset called OPENCODEREASONING for competitive coding. This dataset is used to distill reasoning capabilities from a teacher model (DeepSeek-R1) into smaller student models (Qwen2.5 variants). The distilled SFT-only models achieve state-of-the-art results on LiveCodeBench and CodeContests, even surpassing some models trained with reinforcement learning (RL). The authors analyze the data sources, the impact of code execution filtering, and the importance of instruction/solution diversity. They find that execution filtering negatively affects benchmark accuracy, leading to a preference for instruction diversity. The paper also investigates token efficiency and reasoning patterns of the models.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:**

The paper makes several contributions:

1.  **Large-scale Dataset:** The creation and public release of OPENCODEREASONING, comprising 736,712 samples, represent a substantial contribution to the community. The size is significantly larger than previous reasoning distillation datasets, potentially enabling further research and development. However, the dataset is generated solely through distillation using DeepSeek-R1 and doesn’t include human created data, which limits the diversity of perspectives that LLMs could learn.

2.  **SFT-Only Performance:** The demonstration that SFT alone, using a well-curated dataset, can achieve competitive results compared to RL-based training is significant. This simplifies the training process and makes reasoning capabilities more accessible. Nevertheless, it is difficult to make general statements because the results are achieved with Qwen2.5.

3.  **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the impact of various data curation strategies, particularly the surprising finding that execution filtering *negatively* impacts performance. This counter-intuitive result highlights the importance of instruction diversity over solution correctness, which is valuable for future dataset construction. It challenges the common practice of filtering solely on correctness. However, the reason for the negative impact of execution filtering is not fully elucidated. It is hypothesized that it is due to diversity, but not fully justified or supported by additional evidence.

4.  **Analysis of Reasoning Patterns:** The analysis of reasoning patterns offers some insight into how models approach problem-solving at different difficulty levels. This could inform future research on improving reasoning abilities in LLMs. However, the results here are somewhat incremental, building on existing work and not necessarily leading to breakthrough findings.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependence on a single teacher model (DeepSeek-R1):** The entire dataset is generated by one teacher model. While DeepSeek-R1 is a strong model, this reliance might introduce biases or limitations into the generated dataset. This limits the learning diversity of the LLM and might contribute to decreased generalization.
*   **Limited novelty in analysis techniques:** The paper relies on standard evaluation metrics and analytical methods.
*   **Limited C++ integration:** There are no experiments done that show how the models perform using C++ code. Even though, integration of C++ data enhanced the performance on IOI benchmark, no conclusions can be drawn to say C++ integration is effective overall.

**Significance:**

The paper's main strength is the large-scale reasoning distillation dataset. The empirical findings on the effectiveness of SFT and the importance of instruction diversity are valuable for the community.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the above, the score reflects a solid contribution but falls short of being truly groundbreaking. The dataset itself has high potential, but the weaknesses in the methodology temper its overall impact. The analysis is incremental, and the overall narrative is focused on a single coding-related task. Therefore, considering the strengths and the weaknesses of the contribution,

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### The LLM Wears Prada: Analysing Gender Bias and Stereotypes through Online Shopping Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01951v1)
- **Authors**: Massimiliano Luca, Ciro Beneduce, Bruno Lepri, Jacopo Staiano
- **Abstract**: With the wide and cross-domain adoption of Large Language Models, it becomes crucial to assess to which extent the statistical correlations in training data, which underlie their impressive performance, hide subtle and potentially troubling biases. Gender bias in LLMs has been widely investigated from the perspectives of works, hobbies, and emotions typically associated with a specific gender. In this study, we introduce a novel perspective. We investigate whether LLMs can predict an individual's gender based solely on online shopping histories and whether these predictions are influenced by gender biases and stereotypes. Using a dataset of historical online purchases from users in the United States, we evaluate the ability of six LLMs to classify gender and we then analyze their reasoning and products-gender co-occurrences. Results indicate that while models can infer gender with moderate accuracy, their decisions are often rooted in stereotypical associations between product categories and gender. Furthermore, explicit instructions to avoid bias reduce the certainty of model predictions, but do not eliminate stereotypical patterns. Our findings highlight the persistent nature of gender biases in LLMs and emphasize the need for robust bias-mitigation strategies.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores gender bias in Large Language Models (LLMs) using a novel approach: predicting an individual's gender based solely on their online shopping history. Using a real-world dataset of Amazon purchases, the authors evaluate several LLMs' ability to classify gender and analyze the reasoning behind their predictions. The study reveals that while LLMs achieve moderate accuracy in gender classification, their decisions are often influenced by stereotypical associations between product categories and gender. Explicit instructions to avoid bias reduce the certainty of the models' predictions but do not eliminate stereotypical patterns, highlighting the persistent nature of gender bias in LLMs. Furthermore, the study found that models tend to amplify pre-existing gender biases and stereotypes in certain product categories present in the training data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its application of LLMs to a new domain for bias detection: analyzing online shopping behavior. While gender bias in LLMs is a well-trodden area, the use of real-world purchase data as a proxy for uncovering those biases is a contribution. This is a departure from more common approaches of analyzing pronoun usage or stereotypical job associations. This dataset itself had just been released, but the application to a new domain is certainly novel and important.

*   **Significance:** The findings have implications for recommender systems and other AI applications that rely on inferring user characteristics from their behavior. If LLMs amplify gender biases beyond actual purchasing patterns, they risk misrepresenting user preferences and potentially disadvantaging certain groups. The paper's conclusion that simple bias-mitigation strategies like prompting are insufficient is a significant cautionary note. However, the reliance on a binary gender classification limits the scope of the analysis. The exploration of co-occurrences of product categories and genders provides insights into the specific biases LLMs are encoding, but it also raises questions about the origin of these biases and how they can be addressed more effectively. Another strength is in understanding both the LLMs that are more prone to biases, as well as the types of models with a stronger tendency to elect items associated with female users.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Uses a unique and recently released real-world dataset.
    *   Analyzes the reasoning behind LLM predictions, not just the accuracy.
    *   Demonstrates the limitations of simple bias-mitigation techniques (e.g., prompting).
    *   Identifies specific product categories and their stereotypical associations with gender.
    *   Provides quantitative metrics (JS divergence, F1-score, Jaccard coefficient) to support its claims.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Focuses solely on binary gender classification, neglecting the spectrum of gender identities.
    *   Limited to Amazon purchases, potentially not representative of broader consumer behavior.
    *   Does not explore deeper structural interventions to mitigate bias beyond prompting.
    *   Does not explore the specific interactions with other demographic factors (race, ethnicity, income)

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research on bias detection and mitigation in LLMs. It highlights the importance of using real-world data and analyzing the reasoning behind model predictions. However, its impact may be limited by the fact that it relies on binary gender classification and doesn't propose concrete solutions for mitigating the biases it identifies.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and valuable contribution to the field of bias detection in LLMs by using a unique dataset and focusing on real-world purchasing behavior. It convincingly demonstrates the presence of gender stereotypes and amplification of bias in certain products. While limited by the assumption of a binary gender classification and lack of a novel and effective bias-mitigation technique, the paper makes a strong case for more nuanced analysis of LLM behavior. The paper's emphasis on going beyond accuracy metrics and analyzing the *reasons* behind model predictions has the potential to inspire future research in the area. The novelty of the approach warrants a higher score, but the aforementioned weaknesses prevent it from reaching the top tier.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### VideoScene: Distilling Video Diffusion Model to Generate 3D Scenes in One Step
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01956v2)
- **Authors**: Hanyang Wang, Fangfu Liu, Jiawei Chi, Yueqi Duan
- **Abstract**: Recovering 3D scenes from sparse views is a challenging task due to its inherent ill-posed problem. Conventional methods have developed specialized solutions (e.g., geometry regularization or feed-forward deterministic model) to mitigate the issue. However, they still suffer from performance degradation by minimal overlap across input views with insufficient visual information. Fortunately, recent video generative models show promise in addressing this challenge as they are capable of generating video clips with plausible 3D structures. Powered by large pretrained video diffusion models, some pioneering research start to explore the potential of video generative prior and create 3D scenes from sparse views. Despite impressive improvements, they are limited by slow inference time and the lack of 3D constraint, leading to inefficiencies and reconstruction artifacts that do not align with real-world geometry structure. In this paper, we propose VideoScene to distill the video diffusion model to generate 3D scenes in one step, aiming to build an efficient and effective tool to bridge the gap from video to 3D. Specifically, we design a 3D-aware leap flow distillation strategy to leap over time-consuming redundant information and train a dynamic denoising policy network to adaptively determine the optimal leap timestep during inference. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our VideoScene achieves faster and superior 3D scene generation results than previous video diffusion models, highlighting its potential as an efficient tool for future video to 3D applications. Project Page: https://hanyang-21.github.io/VideoScene
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "VideoScene: Distilling Video Diffusion Model to Generate 3D Scenes in One Step":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VideoScene, a novel framework for generating 3D scenes from sparse (two) views. It addresses the limitations of previous video diffusion models by distilling them into a one-step generation process, thus improving efficiency and 3D consistency. VideoScene employs a 3D-aware leap flow distillation strategy to skip less informative denoising steps, guided by a coarse 3D prior generated using a rapid feed-forward 3DGS model (MVSplat). A dynamic denoising policy network (DDPNet) is also designed to adaptively determine the optimal leap timestep during inference, balancing noise addition and information retention. The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of VideoScene through extensive experiments, showing superior performance in both quality and speed compared to existing video diffusion methods across diverse datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of distilling a video diffusion model for one-step 3D scene generation is novel. Existing approaches leveraging video diffusion priors often suffer from slow inference and lack explicit 3D constraints. The paper's contributions, particularly the 3D-aware leap flow distillation and the dynamic denoising policy network, are key in addressing these limitations. Using a fast feedforward 3DGS model (MVSplat) to provide the 3D geometric structure is a clever choice to address the limitations of the diffusion model that focuses on temporal consistency rather than strict 3D structure.
*   **Significance:** The paper has significant potential impact on the field of 3D reconstruction and scene generation. By achieving faster and more consistent 3D scene generation from sparse views, VideoScene enables wider applicability of these technologies in real-time applications like gaming, robotics, and autonomous navigation. The framework could also facilitate more accessible 3D content creation for casual users. Its also worth noting that the framework is versatile in many 3D scenes reconstruction from video generation models.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Performance:** The experimental results demonstrate that VideoScene outperforms state-of-the-art video diffusion models in both quality and speed. The quantitative and qualitative evaluations across diverse datasets support this claim.
    *   **Efficiency:** The one-step generation capability significantly reduces inference time, addressing a major bottleneck of existing video diffusion methods.
    *   **3D Consistency:** The 3D-aware leap flow distillation strategy and DDPNet contribute to improved geometric consistency and reduced artifacts in the generated scenes.
    *   **Generalization:** The cross-dataset evaluation demonstrates the strong generalizability of VideoScene to novel, out-of-distribution scenes.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on MVSplat:** While the use of MVSplat is a strength, it also introduces a dependency on its performance.  The coarse 3D prior from MVSplat might limit the final output quality in complex scenes where MVSplat itself struggles. The paper could benefit from exploring alternative 3D prior generation methods or addressing the limitations of the initial 3D estimation process.
    *   **Limited Input Views:** Focus on only two input views is a good initial step, but the leap to multi-view reconstruction might not be as straightforward.
    *  **Clarity in ablation Study**: While the qualitative comparisons are strong, the metrics chosen for measuring are subjective. Including more objective metrics could have made a stronger impact in the comparisons.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   VideoScene can serve as a foundation for future research on efficient and consistent 3D scene generation from video data.
    *   The 3D-aware leap flow distillation and dynamic denoising policy network could be adapted to other generative modeling tasks.
    *   The framework's efficiency and accessibility can promote wider adoption of 3D reconstruction and scene generation technologies.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field, it has some limitations. The dependence on a specific 3DGS model (MVSplat) and the focus on only two input views restrict the framework's generality. The clarity of some qualitative comparisons can be further improved. However, the proposed techniques are well-justified, and the experiments provide sufficient evidence of the effectiveness of VideoScene. The improvements in both speed and quality compared to existing methods make this a valuable contribution.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Diffusion-Guided Gaussian Splatting for Large-Scale Unconstrained 3D Reconstruction and Novel View Synthesis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01960v1)
- **Authors**: Niluthpol Chowdhury Mithun, Tuan Pham, Qiao Wang, Ben Southall, Kshitij Minhas, Bogdan Matei, Stephan Mandt, Supun Samarasekera, Rakesh Kumar
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) and Neural Radiance Fields (NeRF) have achieved impressive results in real-time 3D reconstruction and novel view synthesis. However, these methods struggle in large-scale, unconstrained environments where sparse and uneven input coverage, transient occlusions, appearance variability, and inconsistent camera settings lead to degraded quality. We propose GS-Diff, a novel 3DGS framework guided by a multi-view diffusion model to address these limitations. By generating pseudo-observations conditioned on multi-view inputs, our method transforms under-constrained 3D reconstruction problems into well-posed ones, enabling robust optimization even with sparse data. GS-Diff further integrates several enhancements, including appearance embedding, monocular depth priors, dynamic object modeling, anisotropy regularization, and advanced rasterization techniques, to tackle geometric and photometric challenges in real-world settings. Experiments on four benchmarks demonstrate that GS-Diff consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines by significant margins.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GS-Diff, a novel framework that enhances 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) for large-scale, unconstrained 3D reconstruction and novel view synthesis. GS-Diff addresses the limitations of existing 3DGS methods in challenging real-world environments with sparse data, transient occlusions, and appearance variations. It incorporates a multi-view diffusion model to generate pseudo-observations conditioned on input images, effectively transforming the under-constrained 3D reconstruction problem into a better-posed one. The framework also includes several enhancements like appearance embedding, monocular depth priors, dynamic object modeling, anisotropy regularization, and advanced rasterization techniques. Experiments on multiple datasets demonstrate that GS-Diff consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a significant problem:** The paper tackles a crucial limitation of current 3DGS methods, their struggle with large-scale, unconstrained environments, making it more practical.
    *   **Novel Integration of Techniques:** The core novelty lies in the clever combination of a multi-view diffusion model with 3DGS. Using the diffusion model to generate pseudo-views is a valuable strategy for handling sparse and inconsistent input data.
    *   **Comprehensive Enhancements:** The inclusion of various enhancements like appearance embedding and monocular depth priors demonstrates a practical approach to addressing real-world complexities.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper provides compelling experimental results across multiple datasets, showing consistent improvements over existing methods. This supports the effectiveness of GS-Diff.
    *  **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Existing Components:** GS-Diff relies on pre-trained diffusion models (specifically EscherNet) and other existing techniques like Marigold for depth estimation. While integration is itself a contribution, the dependence reduces the fundamental novelty. The effectiveness of GS-Diff is, therefore, bounded by the performance of these individual components.
    *   **Limited Discussion on Computational Cost:** The paper does not delve extensively into the computational cost associated with integrating the diffusion model and other enhancements. This is a critical consideration for real-time applications, a key area of 3DGS.
    *   **Potential for Hallucination:** While the paper attempts to mitigate hallucination issues, the use of a diffusion model inherently carries this risk, especially in highly unconstrained environments. Further analysis and mitigation strategies for these artifacts would strengthen the contribution.
    *   **Object class limitation:** The mask-based dynamic object handling method is limited by the classes that can be recognized by the segmentation model. A more flexible solution would be beneficial.
    *   **Minor improvements for specific scenarios**: Table 1 shows that in dense view sets, the improvement compared to baselines is marginal.

*   **Significance and Novelty:**

    *   The paper has a good level of novelty, particularly in the integration of a diffusion model to guide 3DGS in unconstrained scenarios. The various enhancements tailored to address real-world challenges also add to the novelty.
    *   The paper contributes significantly to making 3DGS applicable to more realistic and challenging scenarios. This has important implications for various applications, including robotics, augmented reality, and virtual tourism.
    *   The work will likely influence future research in this area, encouraging the development of more robust and generalizable 3D reconstruction and novel view synthesis methods.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7.5** to this paper.

*   The core idea of using a diffusion model for guiding 3DGS to address limitations of sparse data and complex scene dynamics is a strong contribution. The suite of enhancements further adds to the practicality and robustness of the method. The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the superiority of GS-Diff over existing methods. The paper addresses a specific, recognized problem in the literature.
*   The paper does not introduce fundamentally new components in the sense that is relies on existing diffusion models and depth estimators. While clever in its integration, this aspect limits the groundbreaking novelty. The lack of comprehensive discussion on computational cost and the potential for hallucinations also detract from the overall impact. I am not assigning a higher score due to the heavy reliance on pre-existing models.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### From Prompts to Templates: A Systematic Prompt Template Analysis for Real-world LLMapps
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02052v1)
- **Authors**: Yuetian Mao, Junjie He, Chunyang Chen
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have revolutionized human-AI interaction by enabling intuitive task execution through natural language prompts. Despite their potential, designing effective prompts remains a significant challenge, as small variations in structure or wording can result in substantial differences in output. To address these challenges, LLM-powered applications (LLMapps) rely on prompt templates to simplify interactions, enhance usability, and support specialized tasks such as document analysis, creative content generation, and code synthesis. However, current practices heavily depend on individual expertise and iterative trial-and-error processes, underscoring the need for systematic methods to optimize prompt template design in LLMapps. This paper presents a comprehensive analysis of prompt templates in practical LLMapps. We construct a dataset of real-world templates from open-source LLMapps, including those from leading companies like Uber and Microsoft. Through a combination of LLM-driven analysis and human review, we categorize template components and placeholders, analyze their distributions, and identify frequent co-occurrence patterns. Additionally, we evaluate the impact of identified patterns on LLMs' instruction-following performance through sample testing. Our findings provide practical insights on prompt template design for developers, supporting the broader adoption and optimization of LLMapps in industrial settings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "From Prompts to Templates: A Systematic Prompt Template Analysis for Real-world LLMapps":

**Summary:**

The paper presents a systematic analysis of prompt templates used in real-world LLM-powered applications (LLMapps).  The authors compile a dataset of prompt templates from open-source LLMapps, including those from companies like Uber and Microsoft.  They use a combination of LLM-driven analysis and human review to categorize template components, placeholders, and their distributions.  The study identifies frequent co-occurrence patterns and evaluates the impact of these patterns on the LLMs' instruction-following performance through sample testing.  The findings provide practical insights and guidelines for developers to design and optimize prompt templates for LLMapps, aiming to improve usability, consistency, and task performance. The authors make their dataset publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While prompt engineering and template design are actively researched areas, the paper introduces novelty by specifically focusing on *prompt templates* within the context of *real-world LLMapps*. Many existing studies analyze generic prompts or synthetic data. Analyzing templates as used in actual applications provides more ecological validity. It is also one of the first papers to propose a thorough analysis about prompt template with real-world applications.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial practical challenge: how to design effective prompt templates for LLMapps. As LLMs become more integrated into software applications, the usability and performance of these applications depend heavily on well-designed templates. By providing a systematic analysis of template components, patterns, and their effects, the paper provides actionable guidelines for developers to improve LLMapp quality and user experience. The released dataset is also a significant contribution for future research.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Real-world Dataset:** The study is grounded in a valuable dataset of real-world prompt templates from diverse LLMapps, increasing the generalizability of findings.
    *   **Systematic Approach:** The combination of LLM-driven analysis and human evaluation provides a rigorous and comprehensive analysis of the data.
    *   **Actionable Insights:** The paper identifies specific patterns and provides concrete recommendations for prompt template design, making it directly applicable to practitioners.
    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The paper explores different components (directive, context, output format etc.), placeholders and how they influence the final output.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Model Dependence:** The LLM-driven analysis, while efficient, relies on the performance of specific LLMs (llama3-70b-8192 and gpt-4o). The identified components or patterns might be influenced by the models' biases or limitations.
    *   **Limited Evaluation:** The sample testing is somewhat limited in scale.  A more extensive evaluation with a wider range of tasks and LLMs could further strengthen the findings.
    *   **Generalizability to different LLMs:** Although the paper used two different LLMs for testing, there are many other LLMs the findings need to be evaluated on.
    *   **Subjective metrics**: Human evaluations of metrics like "content following" is often subjective. It would have been good if the authors had considered an experiment to determine inter-rater reliability.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence the field by:

    *   Providing a foundation for future research on prompt template design and optimization for LLMapps.
    *   Guiding the development of automated prompt template evaluation tools.
    *   Informing best practices for LLM app development.
    *   Serving as a benchmark for future studies in this area.

**Overall:** The paper provides a valuable contribution to the field by systematically analyzing prompt templates in real-world LLMapps. The use of a real-world dataset, the combination of LLM and human analysis, and the actionable insights make this paper a significant step towards improving the design and usability of LLM-powered applications. The weaknesses are mostly related to the limitations inherent in using specific LLMs for analysis and evaluation, but these do not significantly detract from the overall value of the work.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### MageSQL: Enhancing In-context Learning for Text-to-SQL Applications with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02055v1)
- **Authors**: Chen Shen, Jin Wang, Sajjadur Rahman, Eser Kandogan
- **Abstract**: The text-to-SQL problem aims to translate natural language questions into SQL statements to ease the interaction between database systems and end users. Recently, Large Language Models (LLMs) have exhibited impressive capabilities in a variety of tasks, including text-to-SQL. While prior works have explored various strategies for prompting LLMs to generate SQL statements, they still fall short of fully harnessing the power of LLM due to the lack of (1) high-quality contextual information when constructing the prompts and (2) robust feedback mechanisms to correct translation errors. To address these challenges, we propose MageSQL, a text-to-SQL approach based on in-context learning over LLMs. MageSQL explores a suite of techniques that leverage the syntax and semantics of SQL queries to identify relevant few-shot demonstrations as context for prompting LLMs. In particular, we introduce a graph-based demonstration selection method -- the first of its kind in the text-to-SQL problem -- that leverages graph contrastive learning adapted with SQL-specific data augmentation strategies. Furthermore, an error correction module is proposed to detect and fix potential inaccuracies in the generated SQL query. We conduct comprehensive evaluations on several benchmarking datasets. The results show that our proposed methods outperform state-of-the-art methods by an obvious margin.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "MageSQL: Enhancing In-context Learning for Text-to-SQL Applications with Large Language Models" introduces a new framework, MageSQL, to improve the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) in the text-to-SQL task. It addresses two key challenges:  lack of high-quality contextual information in prompts and the absence of robust feedback mechanisms for error correction. MageSQL leverages both syntax and semantics of SQL queries to select relevant few-shot demonstrations. A graph-based demonstration selection method, incorporating graph contrastive learning with SQL-specific data augmentation, is introduced.  An error correction module is also proposed to detect and fix potential inaccuracies in the generated SQL queries. The paper demonstrates improved performance over state-of-the-art methods on benchmark datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Graph-based Demonstration Selection:** The introduction of a graph-based approach for demonstration selection is a significant contribution.  Leveraging graph contrastive learning to capture both structural and semantic information in SQL queries is novel and potentially impactful.  The use of data augmentation strategies specifically tailored to SQL is also a strong point. Previous demonstration selection strategies tended to rely on textual similarity or hardness levels which are simplistic.
    *   **Error Correction Module:** While post-processing is a common technique, the integration of both rule-based and prompt-based error correction is a valuable addition.  The strategy of using LLMs to correct errors, guided by specific rules, is interesting and could be transferable to other tasks.  The choice to use rule-based correction first and then prompting only for complex cases improves efficiency.
    *   **Overall Framework:**  The end-to-end integration of demonstration selection and error correction creates a cohesive system that enhances LLM capabilities for text-to-SQL, which is a positive attribute of the work.

*   **Significance and Impact:**

    *   **Performance Gains:** The reported performance improvements are substantial and demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed techniques.  The results on benchmark datasets (Spider, BIRD) suggest that MageSQL has the potential to advance the state-of-the-art in text-to-SQL. The 13.2% increase in Execution Accuracy (EX) in some cases is very impactful.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The text-to-SQL problem is of significant practical importance for improving database accessibility.  Enhancements to LLM-based solutions have the potential to make database interaction more user-friendly for non-technical users.  The focus on execution accuracy also ensures the practical usability of the generated queries.
    *   **Reproducibility and Generalizability:**  The paper describes the methods in sufficient detail to allow for reproducibility. However, the reliance on OpenAI's GPT-4 and GPT-3.5 APIs may limit reproducibility for researchers without access to these services. It would have been better if the authors had included experiments with open source models. The graph contrastive learning approach could be generalizable to other tasks where structured data representation is important.
    *   **Limitations:** The paper acknowledges the reliance on rule-based heuristics to decide when to apply the prompt-based error correction, which could be a potential area for improvement.  The error analysis is helpful but could be expanded to further investigate the types of errors that MageSQL struggles with. It would have been helpful to see more details on the exact rules used for the rule-based correction module. The reliance on complex prompting also increases the cost of using LLMs. While the authors present a cost analysis, it would be worthwhile to see how the proposed method scales with larger and more complex databases.
    *   **Alternative Methods and Baselines** The authors could have benefited from contrasting the design choices of the method with other graph-based methods and approaches that use self-consistency for error correction.

*   **Overall:**

    The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of text-to-SQL. The proposed MageSQL framework integrates innovative techniques for demonstration selection and error correction, leading to significant performance improvements over existing methods. The graph-based approach and hybrid error correction strategy are particularly noteworthy. Despite some limitations, the paper's findings are well-supported by experimental results and have the potential to influence future research in this area.

Score: 8

*Rationale:*

The score of 8 reflects the paper's clear novelty and importance, supported by strong results. The graph-based demonstration selection and hybrid error correction mechanism are noteworthy contributions that advance the field. However, the reliance on proprietary LLMs and a few unexplored areas (better analysis of failures, more cost and scaling analysis, limited scope of the baseline, better characterization of the rules) prevents it from receiving a higher score. Overall, it is a solid and impactful paper that deserves attention.

- **Classification**: cs.DB
- **Score**: 8/10

### Towards Operationalizing Heterogeneous Data Discovery
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02059v1)
- **Authors**: Jin Wang, Yanlin Feng, Chen Shen, Sajjadur Rahman, Eser Kandogan
- **Abstract**: Querying and exploring massive collections of data sources, such as data lakes, has been an essential research topic in the database community. Although many efforts have been paid in the field of data discovery and data integration in data lakes, they mainly focused on the scenario where the data lake consists of structured tables. However, real-world enterprise data lakes are always more complicated, where there might be silos of multi-modal data sources with structured, semi-structured and unstructured data. In this paper, we envision an end-to-end system with declarative interface for querying and analyzing the multi-modal data lakes. First of all, we come up with a set of multi-modal operators, which is a unified interface that extends the relational operations with AI-composed ones to express analytical workloads over data sources in various modalities. In addition, we formally define the essential steps in the system, such as data discovery, query planning, query processing and results aggregation. On the basis of it, we then pinpoint the research challenges and discuss potential opportunities in realizing and optimizing them with advanced techniques brought by Large Language Models. Finally, we demonstrate our preliminary attempts to address this problem and suggest the future plan for this research topic.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Towards Operationalizing Heterogeneous Data Discovery":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a vision for an end-to-end system that enables declarative querying and analysis of multi-modal data lakes. It addresses the limitations of existing data lake solutions that primarily focus on structured data and argues for a more comprehensive system that can handle structured, semi-structured, and unstructured data. The core of the system is a set of multi-modal operators that extend relational operations with AI-composed ones to express analytical workloads across different data modalities. The paper outlines the essential steps involved, including data discovery, query planning, query processing, and result aggregation. The authors pinpoint research challenges in realizing and optimizing this system, focusing on the potential of large language models (LLMs) to improve various aspects. Finally, they present preliminary attempts to address the problem and suggest future research directions. The proposed system includes a declarative query interface, a data discovery module, a query planning and optimization component, and a verification step.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its proposal for a unified, declarative framework for querying diverse data modalities within a data lake. While individual components (data discovery, query planning, LLM integration) are not entirely new, the combination and formalization of these elements into a cohesive system architecture are a worthwhile contribution. The definition of multi-modal operators that abstract away the specifics of each data source or type is also a novel idea. Existing research tends to focus on specific data types (e.g., tables and text) or relies heavily on LLMs without formally defining the fundamental building blocks. A key strength is in identifying specific areas where LLMs can be integrated not just as a core part of a solution, but as a tool for specific operators in the framework.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge in modern data management: the proliferation of data in diverse formats and locations. The ability to query and analyze this data in a unified way would have significant implications for various domains, including business intelligence, scientific discovery, and data-driven decision-making. While the paper presents a vision rather than a fully implemented system, it clearly articulates the problem and provides a roadmap for future research.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear articulation of the problem:** The paper effectively highlights the limitations of existing data lake solutions in handling heterogeneous data.
*   **Well-defined system architecture:** The proposed architecture is logically structured and addresses the key challenges involved in multi-modal data querying.
*   **Formal definition of multi-modal operators:** The introduction of multi-modal operators provides a unified interface for expressing queries over different data sources.
*   **Identification of research challenges and opportunities:** The paper identifies several open research questions and discusses the potential of LLMs to address these challenges.
*   **Preliminary attempts and future directions:** The initial attempts to benchmark data discovery and the discussion of future research directions provide a valuable starting point for further investigation.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited implementation details:** The paper primarily focuses on the conceptual design of the system and provides limited details on the actual implementation of the various components. The preliminary attempts section is rather weak, and doesn't give much conviction to the feasibility of the complete system.
*   **Lack of evaluation:** While the paper outlines the design of benchmark experiments for data discovery, it lacks a comprehensive evaluation of the overall system performance. The benchmark results are very preliminary and focused only on data discovery aspects.
*   **Over-reliance on LLMs:** While the paper acknowledges the potential of LLMs, it also risks over-relying on these models to solve complex data management challenges. The system's robustness and efficiency might be compromised if LLMs are not carefully integrated.
*   **Limited discussion of data security and governance:** The paper does not adequately address the security and governance aspects of multi-modal data lakes.  Managing access control and ensuring data privacy in such a heterogeneous environment can be extremely complicated.
*   **Scalability concerns:** The paper does not thoroughly explore the scalability challenges of the proposed system, particularly in the context of massive data lakes with thousands of data sources. How would the query planner cope with such complexity? How would metadata be managed effectively?

**Potential Influence:**  Despite the weaknesses, the paper has the potential to influence future research in data management, particularly in the area of data lakes and multi-modal data analysis. The proposed architecture and multi-modal operators can serve as a starting point for developing more comprehensive and efficient data lake solutions. The identification of research challenges and the discussion of LLM integration can stimulate further investigation in these areas. The paper provides a useful framework for reasoning about the complexities involved in heterogeneous data processing and creates a conceptual foundation for future innovations.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **6/10** to this paper. While the paper presents a promising vision and introduces some novel ideas, it lacks concrete implementation details and rigorous evaluation. The system architecture is well-defined, but the absence of a working prototype and extensive experiments limits the practical impact of the research. The over-reliance on LLMs and the lack of discussion on data security and scalability also raise concerns. However, the paper's clear articulation of the problem, formal definition of multi-modal operators, and identification of research challenges make it a valuable contribution to the field. If the authors can address the weaknesses mentioned above and provide a more comprehensive evaluation of the system, the paper's score could be significantly increased.
Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.DB
- **Score**: 6/10

### Aligned Better, Listen Better for Audio-Visual Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02061v1)
- **Authors**: Yuxin Guo, Shuailei Ma, Shijie Ma, Xiaoyi Bao, Chen-Wei Xie, Kecheng Zheng, Tingyu Weng, Siyang Sun, Yun Zheng, Wei Zou
- **Abstract**: Audio is essential for multimodal video understanding. On the one hand, video inherently contains audio, which supplies complementary information to vision. Besides, video large language models (Video-LLMs) can encounter many audio-centric settings. However, existing Video-LLMs and Audio-Visual Large Language Models (AV-LLMs) exhibit deficiencies in exploiting audio information, leading to weak understanding and hallucinations. To solve the issues, we delve into the model architecture and dataset. (1) From the architectural perspective, we propose a fine-grained AV-LLM, namely Dolphin. The concurrent alignment of audio and visual modalities in both temporal and spatial dimensions ensures a comprehensive and accurate understanding of videos. Specifically, we devise an audio-visual multi-scale adapter for multi-scale information aggregation, which achieves spatial alignment. For temporal alignment, we propose audio-visual interleaved merging. (2) From the dataset perspective, we curate an audio-visual caption and instruction-tuning dataset, called AVU. It comprises 5.2 million diverse, open-ended data tuples (video, audio, question, answer) and introduces a novel data partitioning strategy. Extensive experiments show our model not only achieves remarkable performance in audio-visual understanding, but also mitigates potential hallucinations.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Dolphin," a novel audio-visual large language model (AV-LLM) designed to improve audio understanding and mitigate hallucinations, problems commonly observed in existing Video-LLMs and AV-LLMs. Dolphin employs a fine-grained architecture featuring: (1) an audio-visual multi-scale adapter for spatial alignment of audio and visual features at different scales; (2) audio-visual interleaved merging for temporal alignment and interaction between modalities; and (3) a large language model to process the aligned tokens and generate responses. The authors also curate a large-scale audio-visual dataset, AVU, containing 5.2 million samples with captions and question-answer pairs, designed with diverse splits for various training objectives. Experimental results demonstrate that Dolphin achieves significantly improved performance in audio-visual understanding tasks and effectively reduces hallucinations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a critical gap in the development of multimodal language models: the often-underutilized and misunderstood audio modality in video understanding. The idea of creating a dedicated architecture that performs fine-grained spatial and temporal alignment of audio and visual information is well-motivated and has the potential to lead to significantly better performance. The authors attempt to address challenges that plague current AV-LLMs: neglecting the audio modality and generating hallucinations.

*Strengths:*

*   **Well-defined problem and motivation:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of current Video-LLMs and AV-LLMs in handling audio information.
*   **Architectural novelty:** The audio-visual multi-scale adapter and audio-visual interleaved merging are novel architectural components that target specific challenges in audio-visual alignment.
*   **Large-scale dataset:** The AVU dataset is a significant contribution, providing a large and diverse resource for training and evaluating AV-LLMs. The novel data partitioning strategy, focused on different aspects of AV consistency, demonstrates consideration of various training objectives, including negative sampling to mitigate hallucinations.
*   **Experimental results:** The results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of Dolphin in improving audio-visual understanding and reducing hallucinations compared to existing models.
*   **Comprehensive ablations:** Ablation studies thoroughly validate the contributions of individual components and the dataset.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Incremental novelty:** While the proposed components are novel combinations, the underlying techniques (e.g., cross-attention, multi-scale feature extraction) are well-established in the field. The architectural novelty could be seen as more of a clever integration of existing techniques than a fundamental breakthrough. The significance of individual techniques may be overstated.
*   **Dependency on LLM backbones:** Dolphin relies on powerful LLM backbones (Vicuna), the degree to which these models would be more broadly applicable with other more modern models may be less clear.
*   **Lack of a comprehensive theoretical framework:** The paper lacks a comprehensive theoretical justification for the specific architectural choices. While the empirical results are strong, a more rigorous theoretical analysis could strengthen the paper.
*   **Dataset bias:** It is possible that the AVU dataset is biased towards certain types of audio-visual content, which could limit the generalizability of Dolphin. The effectiveness of the approach to reduce hallucinations could have limitations.

*Significance:*

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field of multimodal learning by addressing a practical problem and providing a well-engineered solution. The AVU dataset is a valuable resource that could facilitate further research in AV-LLMs. Dolphin's architectural innovations could inspire new approaches to audio-visual alignment and understanding. Overall, the work makes a moderate contribution to the development of more sophisticated and reliable multimodal AI systems.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper's strengths lie in its practical engineering, strong empirical results, and the creation of a valuable resource. However, the individual components of the architecture, while well-integrated, are not groundbreaking in isolation. There is potential for overstating the contribution of individual techniques, and a deeper theoretical justification could strengthen the paper. The dependence on the base LLM backbone may limit the extensibility of the model with future LLM generations. While the improvement in audio understanding is significant, the paper primarily demonstrates *how* to better leverage existing techniques rather than introducing fundamentally new theoretical insights. Given these considerations, the score reflects the practical utility and tangible improvements achieved, balanced against the incremental nature of the technical contributions.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### From Text to Graph: Leveraging Graph Neural Networks for Enhanced Explainability in NLP
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02064v1)
- **Authors**: Fabio Yáñez-Romero, Andrés Montoyo, Armando Suárez, Yoan Gutiérrez, Ruslan Mitkov
- **Abstract**: Researchers have relegated natural language processing tasks to Transformer-type models, particularly generative models, because these models exhibit high versatility when performing generation and classification tasks. As the size of these models increases, they achieve outstanding results. Given their widespread use, many explainability techniques are developed based on these models. However, this process becomes computationally expensive due to the large size of the models. Additionally, transformers interpret input information through tokens that fragment input words into sequences lacking inherent semantic meaning, complicating the explanation of the model from the very beginning. This study proposes a novel methodology to achieve explainability in natural language processing tasks by automatically converting sentences into graphs and maintaining semantics through nodes and relations that express fundamental linguistic concepts. It also allows the subsequent exploitation of this knowledge in subsequent tasks, making it possible to obtain trends and understand how the model associates the different elements inside the text with the explained task. The experiments delivered promising results in determining the most critical components within the text structure for a given classification.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel approach to enhance explainability in Natural Language Processing (NLP) by converting sentences into graphs that preserve semantic meaning through nodes and relationships representing linguistic concepts. This contrasts with Transformer-based models which tokenize text into sequences lacking inherent semantic meaning, making them harder to interpret.  The methodology involves converting text to constituency trees, then to graphs, fine-tuning a language model to generate embeddings for the graph nodes, training a graph neural network (GNN) to replicate the language model's classifications, and finally using a post-hoc explainability module (SubgraphX) to identify the most critical subgraphs for a given classification task.  The authors apply this methodology to topic classification (AG News) and sentiment analysis (SST-2) datasets, demonstrating promising results in determining the most important text structure components for classification. The paper also explores semantic and structural analysis of the graphs to derive insights into model behavior.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents a compelling argument for shifting the focus of explainability efforts away from the opaque internal workings of large language models (LLMs) toward more structured and interpretable graph representations.  The use of constituency trees to capture semantic relationships before applying a GNN is a notable aspect of this approach. While individual components (GNNs, constituency parsing) are not new, their specific integration for explainability in this manner contributes to novelty. Distilling the LLM knowledge into smaller and explainable GNN models is an interesting concept.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge in NLP: the lack of inherent explainability in large Transformer models. If successful, this methodology could significantly reduce the computational burden of explainability techniques and allow for a more intuitive understanding of how models make decisions. The potential for knowledge graph generation based on identified patterns is another significant contribution. The claim is made that the models have been used to perform well in a few tasks; however, there is a lack of thorough experimentation to backup this claim.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of explainability in LLMs and motivates the need for alternative approaches.
*   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The proposed methodology is detailed and well-structured, with clear explanations of each step.
*   **Emphasis on Semantic Meaning:** The approach prioritizes the preservation of semantic meaning, which is often lost in token-based representations.
*   **Leveraging Graph Structures:** The utilization of graph structures for capturing relationships and enabling explainability is a strong point.
*   **Use of Explainability Module:** Applying post-hoc explainability methods to a trained graph structure with the intent of discovering relations provides a strong understanding for the end user.
*   **Reproducibility:** The authors provide a GitHub repository for replicating their experiments.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Evaluation Datasets:** The evaluation is limited to two datasets (AG News, SST-2), which are relatively simple and might not fully demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach on more complex tasks.
*   **Lack of Comparison to Baseline Explainability Techniques:**  The paper doesn't explicitly compare its approach against existing explainability techniques for Transformer models (e.g., attention visualization, gradient-based methods) on the same datasets. This makes it difficult to assess the relative advantages and disadvantages.
*   **Complexity of the Pipeline:** The proposed methodology involves several steps (constituency parsing, graph conversion, language model fine-tuning, GNN training, explainability module), which might make it difficult to implement and scale to larger datasets or more complex models.
*   **Dependency on Constituency Parsing Accuracy:** The accuracy of the entire pipeline is inherently limited by the accuracy of the initial constituency parsing step. Errors in parsing will propagate through the subsequent steps.
*   **Choice of GNN Architecture and Explainability Technique:** The justification for specifically choosing GCN and SubgraphX is somewhat limited. There are other GNN architectures and explainability methods that might perform better or offer different insights.
*   **Depth of Semantic and Structural Analysis:** While the paper mentions semantic and structural analysis, the discussion of the results is somewhat superficial. A more detailed analysis of the patterns identified in the graphs would strengthen the findings.
*   **Limited scalability experimentation:** The models should be experimented over larger data sets to show their validity in more complex scenarios.

**Potential Influence:** Despite the weaknesses, the paper has the potential to influence the field by offering a viable alternative to the black-box nature of LLMs. It could inspire further research into graph-based explainability techniques and the distillation of knowledge from LLMs into more interpretable models. In addition, the paper needs additional experimentation to prove that its method performs well on more complex scenarios.

**Rigorous Rationale:** The novelty of this technique lies in converting text into constituency trees, using graph structures, using the embeddings in a trained model to identify most important subgraphs, and analyzing relationships within the graphs for explainability. Since the validation sets are low, the model should be tested on more tasks with more data.

**Score:** 6/10

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Evolving Security in LLMs: A Study of Jailbreak Attacks and Defenses
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02080v1)
- **Authors**: Zhengchun Shang, Wenlan Wei
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly popular, powering a wide range of applications. Their widespread use has sparked concerns, especially through jailbreak attacks that bypass safety measures to produce harmful content. In this paper, we present a comprehensive security analysis of large language models (LLMs), addressing critical research questions on the evolution and determinants of model safety. Specifically, we begin by identifying the most effective techniques for detecting jailbreak attacks. Next, we investigate whether newer versions of LLMs offer improved security compared to their predecessors. We also assess the impact of model size on overall security and explore the potential benefits of integrating multiple defense strategies to enhance model robustness. Our study evaluates both open-source models (e.g., LLaMA and Mistral) and closed-source systems (e.g., GPT-4) by employing four state-of-the-art attack techniques and assessing the efficacy of three new defensive approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Evolving Security in LLMs: A Study of Jailbreak Attacks and Defenses":

**Summary:**

The paper presents a comprehensive empirical study on the security of Large Language Models (LLMs) against jailbreak attacks. The study focuses on assessing the impact of model scale, version, and architecture on LLM vulnerability. The authors evaluate various jailbreak attack detection methods, assess the vulnerability of different LLM architectures and versions, and measure the effectiveness of several leading jailbreak defense strategies across different LLMs. The research analyzes both open-source models (LLaMA, Mistral) and closed-source systems (GPT-4), employing four state-of-the-art attack techniques and evaluating the efficacy of three new defensive approaches.  The study aims to provide actionable insights into how LLM design decisions influence vulnerability and how to harden systems against adversarial prompts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Empirical Analysis:** The paper conducts a thorough experimental analysis, covering a variety of LLMs, attacks, and defenses. This breadth is valuable for understanding the landscape of LLM security.
    *   **Systematic Approach:** The methodology is well-defined, and the experiments are designed to systematically address the research questions. The paper examines the interplay of model scale, version, and architecture, providing a multi-faceted perspective.
    *   **Evaluation of Detection Methods:** The paper includes a critical evaluation of different methods to detect jailbreak attempts, which is a crucial step before evaluating defense techniques. This allows for selecting an appropriate and accurate evaluation benchmark (GPT-4o-mini).
    *   **Practical Insights:**  The paper provides practical insights for practitioners, offering evidence-based guidance on defense strategies and the factors influencing LLM vulnerability. The evaluation of latency and resource overhead of defenses is important for real-world deployment.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors commit to providing code, enhancing the reproducibility of the work.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Defenses:**  While the paper evaluates three defense mechanisms, the vast number of existing and emerging defense techniques means that the conclusions may not be universally applicable. The selection of these particular defenses might not capture the full spectrum of effectiveness.
    *   **Dependence on Attack Method Choice:**  The paper acknowledges that the effectiveness of attacks and defenses varies across different methods. While the study covers a few leading techniques, its findings might be sensitive to the specific choice of attacks. Certain attacks may be more effective than others regardless of LLM, skewing the overall results.
    *   **Generalization Concerns:** LLM security is a rapidly evolving field. Conclusions drawn about model versions might not hold in the future due to ongoing improvements in training techniques, safety protocols, and adversarial attack strategies.
    *   **Hyperparameter Limitations:** The paper admits to not exploring the impact of model hyperparameters on safety, which is a valid concern. Different hyperparameter settings (e.g., temperature) could significantly influence model behavior and vulnerability.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper's novelty lies in its comprehensive empirical approach to studying the *evolution* of security in LLMs. Unlike previous surveys or limited evaluations, this study examines how model scale, version, and architecture interact with jailbreak attacks and defenses.
    *   The study's systematic assessment of different LLMs and the evaluation of multiple attack and defense techniques are significant. It fills a gap by providing a holistic view of the security landscape, going beyond conceptual analyses or narrow model evaluations.
    *   The identification of GPT-4o-mini as the most effective and reliable jailbreak attack detection method could influence future research on LLM security, as it provides a benchmark for automated malicious prompt detection.
    *   The paper's findings on the influence of model scale and version on LLM security (or lack thereof) challenge the assumption that larger and newer models are inherently safer. This could affect future LLM development efforts, highlighting the importance of focusing on targeted safety interventions rather than solely relying on scale and version improvements.

*   **Overall Impact:**

    The paper has the potential to influence the LLM security community by providing a comprehensive empirical analysis, identifying key trends, and providing practical insights for practitioners. The study provides evidence-based guidance to defend against increasingly sophisticated adversarial prompts.
