### MD-ProjTex: Texturing 3D Shapes with Multi-Diffusion Projection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02762v1)
- **Authors**: Ahmet Burak Yildirim, Mustafa Utku Aydogdu, Duygu Ceylan, Aysegul Dundar
- **Abstract**: We introduce MD-ProjTex, a method for fast and consistent text-guided texture generation for 3D shapes using pretrained text-to-image diffusion models. At the core of our approach is a multi-view consistency mechanism in UV space, which ensures coherent textures across different viewpoints. Specifically, MD-ProjTex fuses noise predictions from multiple views at each diffusion step and jointly updates the per-view denoising directions to maintain 3D consistency. In contrast to existing state-of-the-art methods that rely on optimization or sequential view synthesis, MD-ProjTex is computationally more efficient and achieves better quantitative and qualitative results.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the MD-ProjTex paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MD-ProjTex, a novel method for generating consistent and high-quality textures for 3D shapes using pre-trained text-to-image diffusion models. The core innovation lies in a multi-diffusion projection technique in UV space, which ensures consistency across multiple viewpoints.  MD-ProjTex fuses noise predictions from different views at each diffusion step, jointly updating denoising directions to maintain 3D consistency.  The method is training-free, doesn't require run-time optimization, and is computationally more efficient than existing state-of-the-art methods that rely on optimization or sequential view synthesis.  The paper presents qualitative and quantitative results demonstrating the superiority of MD-ProjTex over existing approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a genuinely novel approach. It successfully adapts multi-diffusion techniques, primarily used in 2D panorama generation, to the problem of 3D texture generation. The core novelty is the consistent texture generation in UV space leveraging a multi-view framework that is far more performant and consistent than sequential approaches. The specific contributions: multi-diffusion in UV space, encoder-decoder pipeline modifications, modified denoising steps to avoid color saturation, and a normal-guided weighting scheme to select the best views significantly differentiates it.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper is high because it tackles a crucial problem in 3D content creation: generating realistic and controllable textures for 3D models, a complex and generally computationally intensive task. MD-ProjTex provides a fast, efficient, and training-free solution, democratizing access to high-quality 3D texturing.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Performance:** Experimental results clearly demonstrate the improved performance (both in terms of speed and quality) compared to state-of-the-art methods.
    *   **Training-Free:** The fact that it is training-free is a substantial advantage as it does not require large 3D datasets, which are both expensive and difficult to obtain.
    *   **Multi-View Consistency:** The method's strong focus on multi-view consistency directly addresses a critical limitation of many existing texture generation techniques.
    *   **Adaptability:** The demonstrated integration with IP-Adapter shows the method can be easily integrated with new advances in diffusion models.
    *   **Detailed Ablation Studies:** The detailed ablation studies provide a strong understanding of each component's contribution to the final result.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependence on Geometry Quality:** The method's performance is linked to the quality of the input 3D geometry. It can handle imperfect 3D geometry but ultimately depends on its quality for good results.
    *   **Complexity of UV Space and Texture Quality:** The generated texture maps are ultimately bound by the selected UV space and the level of image quality inherent in the underlying image diffusion model used to generate the content being placed in the texture. Better UV mapping tools and image diffusion model versions will likely lead to improved results.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact 3D content creation pipelines, enabling artists and designers to rapidly generate realistic textures for 3D models without extensive training or computational resources. This could lead to wider adoption of 3D modeling across various domains, including gaming, VR/AR, and industrial design.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a compelling and well-executed approach to a challenging problem in 3D content creation. The combination of the novel multi-diffusion projection in UV space, encoder-decoder pipeline modifications, modified denoising steps and normal-guided view selection, together with the significant improvements in performance and multi-view consistency, makes a significant contribution to the field. While the method does rely on input 3D geometry and the limitations of UV space exist, the practical benefits outweigh these shortcomings. The code is also likely relatively simple to implement compared to NERF-based approaches.

### Scene Splatter: Momentum 3D Scene Generation from Single Image with Video Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02764v1)
- **Authors**: Shengjun Zhang, Jinzhao Li, Xin Fei, Hao Liu, Yueqi Duan
- **Abstract**: In this paper, we propose Scene Splatter, a momentum-based paradigm for video diffusion to generate generic scenes from single image. Existing methods, which employ video generation models to synthesize novel views, suffer from limited video length and scene inconsistency, leading to artifacts and distortions during further reconstruction. To address this issue, we construct noisy samples from original features as momentum to enhance video details and maintain scene consistency. However, for latent features with the perception field that spans both known and unknown regions, such latent-level momentum restricts the generative ability of video diffusion in unknown regions. Therefore, we further introduce the aforementioned consistent video as a pixel-level momentum to a directly generated video without momentum for better recovery of unseen regions. Our cascaded momentum enables video diffusion models to generate both high-fidelity and consistent novel views. We further finetune the global Gaussian representations with enhanced frames and render new frames for momentum update in the next step. In this manner, we can iteratively recover a 3D scene, avoiding the limitation of video length. Extensive experiments demonstrate the generalization capability and superior performance of our method in high-fidelity and consistent scene generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Scene Splatter: Momentum 3D Scene Generation from Single Image with Video Diffusion Model" introduces a new paradigm for generating 3D scenes from a single image by leveraging video diffusion models.  The core idea is to use a "momentum-based" approach.  It constructs noisy samples from original image features as a kind of latent-level momentum to enhance details and preserve consistency during the video generation process. Recognizing that latent-level momentum can restrict generation in unseen regions, the method also incorporates a pixel-level momentum derived from a directly generated video (without latent momentum) to improve recovery in these regions.  The paper also discusses iterative refinement using Gaussian representations and finetuning based on the enhanced views.  Experiments are performed to demonstrate the method's high fidelity and consistency in generating 3D scenes compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the "momentum-based" approach, particularly the cascaded combination of latent-level and pixel-level momentum.  This addresses a key challenge in single-image 3D scene generation: balancing generative priors with preserving consistency with the input image, especially in occluded or unseen regions. While other methods have used video diffusion for 3D generation, the specific momentum concept and its implementation seems to be a reasonably original contribution.The iterative scene reconstruction and refinement via gaussian representation also shows improvements in generation of better 3D scenes from single images.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem in computer vision. High-quality and consistent 3D scene generation from a single image has many applications. The results presented show a clear improvement over existing methods, especially in preserving consistency and generating unseen regions. By adapting video diffusion models in a novel way, the work provides a new avenue for research in this area.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing methods.
    *   **Well-Motivated Approach:** The proposed momentum-based approach is well-motivated by the identified weaknesses in existing techniques.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** Both qualitative and quantitative results demonstrate that the method outperforms existing baselines. The ablation studies also provide evidence for the importance of each component of the proposed approach.
    *   **Good Writing Quality:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to understand.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** The reliance on video diffusion models inherently increases the computational cost. The paper acknowledges this as a limitation, but doesn't offer any concrete methods for reducing this cost.
    *   **Dependence on Pre-trained Models:** The method depends on pre-trained diffusion models and other pre-trained networks. The performance of the method relies on the quality of the pre-trained models. This can be problematic if the pre-trained models are not well-suited for the task.
    *   **Limited Generalization Scenarios:** The paper uses real estate datasets to show the effectiveness of the approach. While real estate data is a good dataset for testing, it doesn't fully represent the variety of scenarios for 3D generation.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of 3D scene generation from single images. The "momentum-based" framework is novel and provides a promising direction for future research. The experimental results are convincing and demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method. However, the relatively high computational cost and dependence on pre-trained models limits its immediate applicability in real-time scenarios. While the quantitative data supports the claim, the range of experiments could be made much more robust. The 3D scene generation seems limited only to indoor settings.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### How Deep Do Large Language Models Internalize Scientific Literature and Citation Practices?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02767v1)
- **Authors**: Andres Algaba, Vincent Holst, Floriano Tori, Melika Mobini, Brecht Verbeken, Sylvia Wenmackers, Vincent Ginis
- **Abstract**: The spread of scientific knowledge depends on how researchers discover and cite previous work. The adoption of large language models (LLMs) in the scientific research process introduces a new layer to these citation practices. However, it remains unclear to what extent LLMs align with human citation practices, how they perform across domains, and may influence citation dynamics. Here, we show that LLMs systematically reinforce the Matthew effect in citations by consistently favoring highly cited papers when generating references. This pattern persists across scientific domains despite significant field-specific variations in existence rates, which refer to the proportion of generated references that match existing records in external bibliometric databases. Analyzing 274,951 references generated by GPT-4o for 10,000 papers, we find that LLM recommendations diverge from traditional citation patterns by preferring more recent references with shorter titles and fewer authors. Emphasizing their content-level relevance, the generated references are semantically aligned with the content of each paper at levels comparable to the ground truth references and display similar network effects while reducing author self-citations. These findings illustrate how LLMs may reshape citation practices and influence the trajectory of scientific discovery by reflecting and amplifying established trends. As LLMs become more integrated into the scientific research process, it is important to understand their role in shaping how scientific communities discover and build upon prior work.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and rigorous evaluation of the provided paper, along with a score and justification:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates how large language models (LLMs) internalize scientific literature and influence citation practices. It finds that LLMs tend to reinforce the "Matthew effect" by favoring highly cited papers and that this pattern persists across scientific domains. While LLM-generated references demonstrate semantic alignment with focal papers, they also exhibit biases toward more recent publications, shorter titles, and fewer authors. The paper suggests that LLMs could reshape citation practices by reflecting and amplifying existing trends in scientific literature.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty:** The paper tackles a highly relevant question about the integration of LLMs into scientific workflows and their potential impact on knowledge dissemination.  Analyzing the inherent biases of LLMs in citation generation is a relatively underexplored area.
    *   **Methodology:**  The paper employs a rigorous methodology by comparing LLM-generated citations with human-authored citations. The study controls for potential confounds (e.g., the number of references in a paper) and explores the results across different scientific domains.
    *   **Scope:** The analysis of a large dataset (274,951 references from 10,000 papers) strengthens the findings and supports their generalizability.
    *   **Relevance:** The findings have direct implications for researchers using LLMs for literature reviews, scientific synthesis, and other knowledge discovery tasks. Understanding these biases is crucial for responsible use of LLMs in science.
    *   **Significance:** The paper highlights the potential for LLMs to both aid and distort scientific discovery by influencing citation dynamics and amplifying existing biases.  This is significant because it points to the need for careful design and evaluation of AI tools in science.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Experimental setup limitations:** The LLM prompting is relatively simple (title, authors, year, venue, abstract) and it is important to note the study isolates the LLM's *parametric* knowledge, i.e. knowledge learned during training, which may limit a generalizability of the conclusions in the real-world. Real-world usage would likely involve more interactive prompting and external data sources.
    *   **Existence Check Conservative Method:** The method of checking the "existence" of a cited work is quite strict (requiring very high title similarity); this may underestimate the LLM's accuracy and lead to overestimation of biases.
    *   **Limited scope of citation features:** While the paper examines several citation features (e.g., recency, author count), other relevant factors (e.g., author gender, institutional affiliation) could further illuminate LLM biases.

*   **Novelty and Significance:** The research is significant. It identifies the specific citation pattern distortions that a LLM, GPT4-o produces, and can be used to improve future LLM usage.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research on LLMs in science, particularly related to bias mitigation, responsible AI design, and understanding the long-term effects of AI on knowledge dissemination. The findings could also inform best practices for using LLMs in literature reviews and scientific synthesis.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The paper provides sufficient data and justifications to support its claims. The statistical analyses and comparison with human citation practices give credibility to the conclusions. However, the previously mentioned potential overestimation of bias and limited scope of experiment design should be accounted for.

**Score: 8**

The paper demonstrates a significant contribution to understanding the influence of LLMs on scientific citation practices and knowledge dissemination. The study's rigor, relevance, and potential influence warrant a high score, although the impact is reduced due to the inherent limitations in isolating the LLM's parametric knowledge, potentially overestimating citation pattern distortions, and neglecting relevant citation features.

- **Classification**: cs.DL
- **Score**: 8/10

### BT-ACTION: A Test-Driven Approach for Modular Understanding of User Instruction Leveraging Behaviour Trees and LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02779v1)
- **Authors**: Alexander Leszczynski, Sarah Gillet, Iolanda Leite, Fethiye Irmak Dogan
- **Abstract**: Natural language instructions are often abstract and complex, requiring robots to execute multiple subtasks even for seemingly simple queries. For example, when a user asks a robot to prepare avocado toast, the task involves several sequential steps. Moreover, such instructions can be ambiguous or infeasible for the robot or may exceed the robot's existing knowledge. While Large Language Models (LLMs) offer strong language reasoning capabilities to handle these challenges, effectively integrating them into robotic systems remains a key challenge. To address this, we propose BT-ACTION, a test-driven approach that combines the modular structure of Behavior Trees (BT) with LLMs to generate coherent sequences of robot actions for following complex user instructions, specifically in the context of preparing recipes in a kitchen-assistance setting. We evaluated BT-ACTION in a comprehensive user study with 45 participants, comparing its performance to direct LLM prompting. Results demonstrate that the modular design of BT-ACTION helped the robot make fewer mistakes and increased user trust, and participants showed a significant preference for the robot leveraging BT-ACTION. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/1Eggbert7/BT_LLM.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary of the paper, followed by a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance, and assign a score.

**Summary of the Paper**

The paper "BT-ACTION: A Test-Driven Approach for Modular Understanding of User Instruction Leveraging Behaviour Trees and LLMs" presents a novel approach (BT-ACTION) for enabling robots to understand and execute complex user instructions in a kitchen-assistance setting. The core idea is to combine Behavior Trees (BTs) with Large Language Models (LLMs) in a modular way.  BT-ACTION first classifies user instructions (e.g., clear, ambiguous, modifiable, or infeasible) using LLMs within BT condition nodes.  Based on the classification, different branches of the BT are activated: clear instructions lead to direct task execution; ambiguous instructions trigger clarification questions; modifiable instructions prompt LLM to generate new action sequences; and infeasible requests result in explanations to the user. Crucially, BT-ACTION incorporates error-handling mechanisms (KNOWNO and CheckNewSeq) to address LLM hallucinations and ensure safety. The system was evaluated in a user study with 45 participants, comparing it to a baseline system that directly prompts the LLM.  The results indicated that BT-ACTION reduced task errors, increased user trust, and was preferred by the participants.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance**

This paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of human-robot interaction, specifically in the area of instruction understanding and task execution.  The use of LLMs in HRI is not entirely novel; however, the authors' approach of integrating LLMs *within* a Behavior Tree architecture is a significant step forward.  Here's a breakdown of the key strengths and weaknesses, influencing the novelty and significance:

**Strengths:**

*   **Modular Design:** The combination of BTs and LLMs is not merely a superficial integration. The authors leverage the hierarchical and modular nature of BTs to structure the interaction and handle different scenarios effectively. The ability to independently test and develop each node in the BT contributes to a more explainable and predictable system.
*   **Test-Driven Approach:** The paper emphasizes a test-driven development process, which is crucial for building robust and reliable robotic systems.  Starting with simple LLM prompts and iteratively refining them based on a case dataset demonstrates a practical and systematic approach.
*   **Error Handling:** The inclusion of explicit error-handling mechanisms (KNOWNO and CheckNewSeq) is a major strength.  LLMs are known to hallucinate, so incorporating methods to detect and mitigate these errors is essential for real-world applications. These mechanisms are critical in raising user trust.
*   **User Study:** A comprehensive user study with a reasonably large participant pool (45) provides solid empirical evidence to support the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The comparison to a direct LLM prompting baseline highlights the benefits of the BT-ACTION framework.
*   **Focus on Clarification and Explanation:** The system's ability to clarify ambiguous requests and explain infeasible actions improves the user experience and builds trust. This aligns with principles of explainable AI (XAI) in HRI.
*   **Well-defined Evaluation Metrics:** The authors consider diverse metrics to asses the interaction performance, encompassing both subjective and objective dimensions.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Generalizability:** While the kitchen-assistant scenario is relevant, the paper could benefit from a discussion about the generalizability of BT-ACTION to other domains.  The prompts and knowledge base are likely specific to the kitchen environment, and adapting the system to a different task might require significant effort.
*   **Dependency on LLM Performance:** The system's performance is inherently tied to the capabilities of the underlying LLM (ChatGPT-40-mini in this case). Improvements (or regressions) in LLM technology will directly affect BT-ACTION's behavior. The study was also done under very controlled conditions. A more in-the-wild study could reveal more insight into the potential challenges for the BT-ACTION to generalize.
*   **KNOWNO Framework Dependency:** The KNOWNO framework is used to implement one of the error-handling mechanisms. However, the study does not provide details on KNOWNO framework and its limitations.

**Novelty:**

The specific combination of a modular Behavior Tree architecture, with LLMs used at specific condition and action nodes combined with specific error handling methods (KNOWNO) adds a layer of novelty to the work. While LLMs and BTs have been used individually in HRI, the way they are combined here to address specific challenges in instruction following is a valuable contribution.

**Significance:**

The significance of this work lies in its potential to improve the reliability and usability of robots that interact with humans through natural language. By addressing the limitations of LLMs through structured reasoning and error handling, BT-ACTION makes it more feasible to deploy these robots in real-world environments. The improved user trust and preference observed in the study suggests that this approach can lead to more positive and effective human-robot interactions. The open-source availability of the code will further enhance the impact by enabling other researchers to build upon this work.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

I am assigning a score of 7.5 because:

*   The paper demonstrates a well-designed and evaluated approach to a relevant problem in HRI.
*   The modular architecture and error-handling mechanisms are key strengths.
*   The user study provides empirical evidence to support the claims.
*   The dependency on LLMs and the specific kitchen scenario limit the generalizability to some extent.
*   The open-sourcing of code increases the practical value of the work.
*   The use of the KNOWNO frame-work needs to be further detailed.

While the individual components (BTs and LLMs) are not entirely new, the specific way in which they are combined, the focus on error handling, and the emphasis on user experience through clarification and explanation make this a valuable contribution to the field, justifying the score assigned.

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### From Consumption to Collaboration: Measuring Interaction Patterns to Augment Human Cognition in Open-Ended Tasks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02780v1)
- **Authors**: Joshua Holstein, Moritz Diener, Philipp Spitzer
- **Abstract**: The rise of Generative AI, and Large Language Models (LLMs) in particular, is fundamentally changing cognitive processes in knowledge work, raising critical questions about their impact on human reasoning and problem-solving capabilities. As these AI systems become increasingly integrated into workflows, they offer unprecedented opportunities for augmenting human thinking while simultaneously risking cognitive erosion through passive consumption of generated answers. This tension is particularly pronounced in open-ended tasks, where effective solutions require deep contextualization and integration of domain knowledge. Unlike structured tasks with established metrics, measuring the quality of human-LLM interaction in such open-ended tasks poses significant challenges due to the absence of ground truth and the iterative nature of solution development. To address this, we present a framework that analyzes interaction patterns along two dimensions: cognitive activity mode (exploration vs. exploitation) and cognitive engagement mode (constructive vs. detrimental). This framework provides systematic measurements to evaluate when LLMs are effective tools for thought rather than substitutes for human cognition, advancing theoretical understanding and practical guidance for developing AI systems that protect and augment human cognitive capabilities.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "From Consumption to Collaboration: Measuring Interaction Patterns to Augment Human Cognition in Open-Ended Tasks."

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the critical challenge of understanding and measuring human-LLM interaction in open-ended tasks, where the goal is to augment human cognition rather than simply replace it. The authors propose a framework that analyzes interaction patterns along two dimensions: cognitive activity mode (exploration vs. exploitation) and cognitive engagement mode (constructive vs. detrimental). They argue that effective human-LLM collaboration requires a balance between exploring new possibilities and exploiting existing solutions, and, crucially, that the engagement should be constructive, involving critical evaluation and integration of LLM outputs rather than passive consumption. The paper presents a methodology for measuring these interaction patterns, including qualitative analysis and a proposed automated analysis using LLMs. Finally, it illustrates the application of the framework with an example dialogue.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its conceptual framework for analyzing human-LLM interaction in open-ended tasks.  Prior work has focused more on structured tasks or on the effectiveness of AI explanations.  The two-dimensional framework (exploration/exploitation vs. constructive/detrimental) offers a more nuanced view of how humans engage with LLMs and identifies potential pitfalls in the interaction process. The emphasis on distinguishing between 'consumption' and genuine 'collaboration' is a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the growing importance of LLMs in knowledge work and the potential risks of over-reliance and cognitive erosion. By providing a framework for measuring and understanding interaction patterns, the paper offers a way to design and evaluate LLM systems that genuinely augment human cognition. This has significant implications for the design of tools for thought, learning environments, and other applications where LLMs are used to support complex problem-solving. The paper also correctly identifies the need for interventions to prevent users from falling into the trap of detrimental engagement.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Conceptual Framework:**  The two-dimensional framework is well-defined and provides a useful lens for analyzing human-LLM interaction.
    *   **Practical Measurement Approach:** The paper proposes a systematic approach for measuring interaction patterns, including qualitative analysis and a potential automated analysis using LLMs. This provides a concrete path for researchers to apply the framework in practice.
    *   **Relevant Example:** The example dialogue effectively illustrates the application of the framework and highlights the key interaction patterns.
    *   **Addresses an Important Problem:** The paper tackles a relevant and important problem in the field of human-AI interaction, namely how to ensure that LLMs are used to augment rather than diminish human cognitive capabilities.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Empirical Validation:** The paper's primary weakness is the lack of comprehensive empirical validation. The illustrative example is helpful, but it is not sufficient to demonstrate the generalizability and reliability of the proposed measurement approach.
    *   **Reliance on Qualitative Analysis:** While the qualitative analysis is valuable, it is also subjective and time-consuming.  The proposed automated analysis using LLMs is promising, but it requires further development and validation to ensure its accuracy and reliability. The criteria for judging constructive vs detrimental engagement, while well-defined conceptually, may be difficult to reliably assess in practice.
    *   **Lack of Specific Design Interventions:** While the paper identifies the importance of designing LLM systems that foster constructive engagement, it does not offer specific design interventions beyond general principles. Future work should focus on developing concrete design recommendations based on the framework.
    *   **Limited Discussion of Contextual Factors:** The paper does not fully address the role of contextual factors such as user expertise, task complexity, and domain knowledge in shaping human-LLM interaction patterns.  These factors are likely to influence the effectiveness of the framework and should be considered in future research.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design and evaluation of LLM systems for cognitive support. The framework could be used to develop new metrics for evaluating the effectiveness of human-LLM interaction and to design interventions that promote constructive engagement. The idea of automated pattern analysis could streamline the process of evaluating system designs, facilitating more iterative development.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of human-AI interaction by providing a novel conceptual framework for analyzing human-LLM interaction in open-ended tasks. While the lack of comprehensive empirical validation and specific design interventions limits its impact, the paper offers a promising direction for future research and has the potential to influence the design of LLM systems that genuinely augment human cognition. The clarity of the framework and the practical measurement approach make it a useful tool for researchers and practitioners alike. A score of 7 reflects the paper's novelty and significance, balanced by its limitations in empirical validation and specific design recommendations.

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### GPT-ImgEval: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Diagnosing GPT4o in Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02782v1)
- **Authors**: Zhiyuan Yan, Junyan Ye, Weijia Li, Zilong Huang, Shenghai Yuan, Xiangyang He, Kaiqing Lin, Jun He, Conghui He, Li Yuan
- **Abstract**: The recent breakthroughs in OpenAI's GPT4o model have demonstrated surprisingly good capabilities in image generation and editing, resulting in significant excitement in the community. This technical report presents the first-look evaluation benchmark (named GPT-ImgEval), quantitatively and qualitatively diagnosing GPT-4o's performance across three critical dimensions: (1) generation quality, (2) editing proficiency, and (3) world knowledge-informed semantic synthesis. Across all three tasks, GPT-4o demonstrates strong performance, significantly surpassing existing methods in both image generation control and output quality, while also showcasing exceptional knowledge reasoning capabilities. Furthermore, based on the GPT-4o's generated data, we propose a classification-model-based approach to investigate the underlying architecture of GPT-4o, where our empirical results suggest the model consists of an auto-regressive (AR) combined with a diffusion-based head for image decoding, rather than the VAR-like architectures. We also provide a complete speculation on GPT-4o's overall architecture. In addition, we conduct a series of analyses to identify and visualize GPT-4o's specific limitations and the synthetic artifacts commonly observed in its image generation. We also present a comparative study of multi-round image editing between GPT-4o and Gemini 2.0 Flash, and discuss the safety implications of GPT-4o's outputs, particularly their detectability by existing image forensic models. We hope that our work can offer valuable insight and provide a reliable benchmark to guide future research, foster reproducibility, and accelerate innovation in the field of image generation and beyond. The codes and datasets used for evaluating GPT-4o can be found at https://github.com/PicoTrex/GPT-ImgEval.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GPT-ImgEval, a new benchmark for evaluating the image generation capabilities of OpenAI's GPT-4o model.  The benchmark focuses on three key dimensions: generation quality (using GenEval), instruction-based image editing (using Reason-Edit), and world knowledge-informed semantic synthesis (using WISE). The authors evaluate GPT-4o across these tasks, comparing its performance to existing methods. The paper also explores potential architectural choices within GPT-4o by training a classifier to distinguish between images generated by diffusion-based or autoregressive models. Finally, the paper discusses limitations of GPT-4o, compares it with Gemini 2.0 Flash, and assesses the detectability of GPT-4o-generated images using forensic models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a timely and relevant contribution by providing one of the first comprehensive evaluations of GPT-4o's image generation abilities. Given the significant hype and the widespread adoption of GPT-4o, the paper addresses a crucial need for rigorous, quantitative analysis. The exploration of the underlying architecture through a classifier-based approach, while not definitive proof, represents a novel methodological approach. The comparison to Gemini 2.0 Flash provides further context to GPT-4o's capabilities. The work also attempts to reveal the weaknesses of GPT-4o in detail. This offers a practical understanding of the capabilities and limitations of the model.

*   **Significance:** The benchmark and evaluation are significant for several reasons. First, they provide a concrete basis for comparing GPT-4o to other image generation models. Second, they offer insights into the model's strengths and weaknesses, potentially guiding future research and development efforts.  Third, the discussions on safety and detectability highlight important ethical considerations.  The identification of weaknesses and failure modes are particularly valuable. By making the code and datasets publicly available, the paper fosters reproducibility and collaboration within the field.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The benchmark covers a broad range of image generation tasks, offering a well-rounded assessment of GPT-4o's capabilities.
    *   **Quantitative and Qualitative Analysis:**  The paper combines quantitative metrics with qualitative examples, providing a more nuanced understanding of the model's performance.
    *   **Architectural Exploration:** The attempt to infer GPT-4o's internal architecture, while speculative, adds an interesting dimension to the analysis.
    *   **Practical Insights:** The detailed analysis of limitations and failure cases offers practical guidance for users and developers.
    *   **Open and Reproducible:** The sharing of code and datasets promotes further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Architectural Speculation:** The architectural analysis remains largely speculative due to the lack of direct access to GPT-4o's internal workings.  The classifier-based approach can only provide suggestive, rather than conclusive, evidence.
    *   **Limited Forensic Analysis:** While the paper addresses the detectability of generated images, a more in-depth forensic analysis could provide stronger conclusions regarding safety concerns.
    *  **GPT-4o API access:** the access to image generation models via API is still limited. This reduces the reproducibility of the results.
    *   **Lack of novelty in benchmark design:** the benchmark used (GPT-ImgEval) uses standard evaluation measures.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The GPT-ImgEval benchmark is likely to be adopted by other researchers in the field, serving as a standard for evaluating future image generation models.  The identified limitations may inspire research aimed at addressing these weaknesses. The insights into potential architectural choices could guide model design. The ethical considerations raised will contribute to ongoing discussions about responsible AI development.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**  The paper provides a valuable and timely contribution to the field by offering a first look at the image generation capabilities of GPT-4o. The comprehensive benchmark, the identification of weaknesses, and the architectural speculations are all significant contributions. However, the speculative nature of the architectural analysis and limited API access prevent it from receiving a higher score. The paper is not groundbreaking in terms of methodology but is significant due to the importance of the evaluated model. GPT-4o is one of the most useful AI models and the analyses conducted here are helpful for future research.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Framework for Robust Cognitive Evaluation of LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02789v1)
- **Authors**: Karin de Langis, Jong Inn Park, Bin Hu, Khanh Chi Le, Andreas Schramm, Michael C. Mensink, Andrew Elfenbein, Dongyeop Kang
- **Abstract**: Emergent cognitive abilities in large language models (LLMs) have been widely observed, but their nature and underlying mechanisms remain poorly understood. A growing body of research draws on cognitive science to investigate LLM cognition, but standard methodologies and experimen-tal pipelines have not yet been established. To address this gap we develop CognitivEval, a framework for systematically evaluating the artificial cognitive capabilities of LLMs, with a particular emphasis on robustness in response collection. The key features of CognitivEval include: (i) automatic prompt permutations, and (ii) testing that gathers both generations and model probability estimates. Our experiments demonstrate that these features lead to more robust experimental outcomes. Using CognitivEval, we replicate five classic experiments in cognitive science, illustrating the framework's generalizability across various experimental tasks and obtaining a cognitive profile of several state of the art LLMs. CognitivEval will be released publicly to foster broader collaboration within the cognitive science community.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "A Framework for Robust Cognitive Evaluation of LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces COGNITIVEVAL, a framework designed to systematically evaluate the cognitive capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). It addresses the need for more robust methodologies in LLM cognition research, particularly focusing on robustness in response collection. COGNITIVEVAL's key features include: automatic prompt permutations to mitigate prompt-specific biases and testing mechanisms that gather both LLM-generated responses and model probability estimates for a more nuanced understanding of model confidence. The authors replicate five classic cognitive science experiments using COGNITIVEVAL, demonstrating its generalizability across different tasks and obtaining cognitive profiles of several state-of-the-art LLMs. The results highlight LLMs' strengths in short-term memory but weaknesses in working memory and executive functions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the integration of existing techniques into a cohesive and readily usable framework specifically targeted at the cognitive evaluation of LLMs.  Automatic prompt permutation is known, as is the measurement of token probabilities.  The contribution is the packaging and demonstration of these techniques in a single system with a focus on robustness. While other work has explored cognitive tasks in LLMs, COGNITIVEVAL provides a reusable infrastructure.  It facilitates easier replication, comparison, and extension of existing research. The inclusion of both response generation and probability estimation is a good feature.

* **Significance:**  The significance of the paper is that it addresses a key gap in the field: the lack of standardized methodologies for evaluating LLM cognition.  By offering COGNITIVEVAL as a publicly available tool, the authors aim to foster broader collaboration and more reliable research findings within the community. The ability to easily run a variety of cognitive science experiments on different LLMs in a standardized way allows for systematic comparisons and can contribute to the development of more grounded theories of LLM "cognition" (or lack thereof). The replication of classic experiments provides a valuable benchmark for future LLM development and evaluation. The observation of LLM sensitivity to prompt variations reinforces the importance of prompt engineering and robust experimental design.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Practical tool:** COGNITIVEVAL offers a practical, readily usable framework that can lower the barrier to entry for researchers interested in exploring LLM cognition.
    *   **Focus on robustness:** The emphasis on prompt permutation and dual metric collection (responses and probabilities) enhances the reliability of experimental results.
    *   **Demonstration through replication:**  Replicating well-established cognitive science experiments provides a strong validation of the framework's capabilities.
    *   **Clear presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the design and use of COGNITIVEVAL.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental innovation:**  The core techniques are not entirely novel. The contribution rests more in the *integration* of these into a single workflow and tool.
    *   **Limited cognitive scope:** The paper focuses on a relatively narrow range of cognitive abilities (memory and executive function). While this provides depth, it limits the overall scope of the framework's demonstrated applicability.
    *   **Interpretation of LLM “cognition”:** The paper, while careful, still uses terms like "cognition" and "memory" somewhat liberally. While useful for framing, a more critical discussion of the limitations of mapping human cognitive constructs onto LLM processes would strengthen the argument. Just because an LLM can mimic a behavior related to a cognitive process, doesn't mean it is using a similar (or even comparable) process.
    *   **Reliance on existing tasks:** The framework's utility is contingent on the continued relevance and validity of the classic cognitive science experiments it incorporates.

*   **Potential Influence:**  COGNITIVEVAL has the potential to influence the field by promoting more rigorous and standardized evaluations of LLM cognition. It can serve as a foundation for future research, facilitating the development of more sophisticated models and theories of LLM behavior. However, its influence will depend on its adoption and use within the community.

**Overall:**

COGNITIVEVAL is a valuable contribution because it addresses a clear need for standardized and robust methodologies in the evaluation of LLM cognition. While its core techniques are not radically new, the framework's integration of these elements and its user-friendly design make it a significant step forward. Its influence will be determined by its adoption and use by other researchers in the field.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper provides a useful, practical tool that addresses a key need in the field.  It's more of an engineering contribution, providing infrastructure to ease the application of cognitive science techniques to LLM evaluation, than a theoretical breakthrough.  While not exceptionally groundbreaking in its individual components, the integration and potential for community adoption justify a solid "7".

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Spline-based Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02797v1)
- **Authors**: Prashanth Chandran, Agon Serifi, Markus Gross, Moritz Bächer
- **Abstract**: We introduce Spline-based Transformers, a novel class of Transformer models that eliminate the need for positional encoding. Inspired by workflows using splines in computer animation, our Spline-based Transformers embed an input sequence of elements as a smooth trajectory in latent space. Overcoming drawbacks of positional encoding such as sequence length extrapolation, Spline-based Transformers also provide a novel way for users to interact with transformer latent spaces by directly manipulating the latent control points to create new latent trajectories and sequences. We demonstrate the superior performance of our approach in comparison to conventional positional encoding on a variety of datasets, ranging from synthetic 2D to large-scale real-world datasets of images, 3D shapes, and animations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Spline-based Transformers":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Spline-based Transformers, a novel architecture that eliminates the need for positional encoding in traditional Transformers. Instead of positional encoding, the method embeds input sequences as smooth trajectories in a latent space, using splines defined by learned control points. The authors argue that this approach overcomes limitations of positional encoding like sequence length extrapolation and provides a new way to interact with transformer latent spaces by manipulating the spline control points. They demonstrate superior performance compared to conventional positional encoding on a range of datasets, including synthetic 2D data, images, 3D shapes, and animations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of using splines to represent sequences in the latent space of a transformer is innovative. Positional encoding has been a long-standing area of research in the transformer community, and offering a viable alternative that addresses some of its inherent limitations is a meaningful contribution. The specific application of spline interpolation within a transformer's latent space distinguishes it from other attempts to improve or replace positional encodings. While splines themselves are not new, their integration into this context constitutes a novel contribution. The ability to directly manipulate the latent space through control point adjustments is a further unique feature.

* **Significance:** The potential impact is significant, though the degree will depend on the broader adoption and further development of the method. If Spline-based Transformers prove more robust to sequence length variations and offer intuitive latent space control, it could influence how transformers are designed and applied across various domains. The experimental results, showing improved performance on diverse datasets, provide encouraging evidence of its potential.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of positional encoding (extrapolation, redundancy).
    * **Novel Approach:** The spline-based approach is a well-motivated and creatively implemented alternative.
    * **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper provides compelling evidence of improved performance across various datasets.
    * **Latent Space Control:** The ability to manipulate the spline control points provides a novel means of interacting with the model's latent representations.
    * **Well-Written and Organized:** The paper is easy to follow and understand.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Sensitivity to Hyperparameters:** The paper acknowledges that the Spline-based Transformers are sensitive to learning rates and other hyperparameters, suggesting potential instability or difficulty in tuning. More robust training procedures are needed.
    * **Limited theoretical analysis:** While empirically strong, the paper could benefit from more theoretical analysis that helps elucidate the advantages of using spline based interpolation. In particular, a discussion of any inductive biases introduced by splines would strengthen the work.
    * **Scalability concerns:** The paper lacks discussion about the scalability and computational complexity as the number of control points are increased.
    * **Limited Comparison to More Recent Techniques:** While ALiBi is a good baseline, comparing to some of the very latest relative positional encoding techniques (beyond what is cited) would strengthen the paper.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in transformer architectures and latent space manipulation. It could lead to new approaches for handling sequential data and interacting with learned representations.

* **Overall Assessment:** The paper presents a well-executed and novel approach to a significant problem in the transformer field. The empirical results are promising, and the concept of latent space manipulation offers exciting possibilities. However, the acknowledged limitations (hyperparameter sensitivity) and the need for further analysis suggest that more work is needed to fully realize the potential of this approach.

### A Survey of Large Language Models in Mental Health Disorder Detection on Social Media
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02800v1)
- **Authors**: Zhuohan Ge, Nicole Hu, Darian Li, Yubo Wang, Shihao Qi, Yuming Xu, Han Shi, Jason Zhang
- **Abstract**: The detection and intervention of mental health issues represent a critical global research focus, and social media data has been recognized as an important resource for mental health research. However, how to utilize Large Language Models (LLMs) for mental health problem detection on social media poses significant challenges. Hence, this paper aims to explore the potential of LLM applications in social media data analysis, focusing not only on the most common psychological disorders such as depression and anxiety but also incorporating psychotic disorders and externalizing disorders, summarizing the application methods of LLM from different dimensions, such as text data analysis and detection of mental disorders, and revealing the major challenges and shortcomings of current research. In addition, the paper provides an overview of popular datasets, and evaluation metrics. The survey in this paper provides a comprehensive frame of reference for researchers in the field of mental health, while demonstrating the great potential of LLMs in mental health detection to facilitate the further application of LLMs in future mental health interventions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a survey of the application of Large Language Models (LLMs) to the detection of mental health disorders using social media data.  It covers a range of disorders, including depression, anxiety, psychotic disorders, and externalizing disorders. The survey discusses various LLM applications, research methods, datasets, and evaluation metrics. It also outlines current limitations, challenges, and future research directions in the field, emphasizing the potential of LLMs to aid in early detection and intervention for mental health issues.  The paper highlights the strengths of LLMs in processing unstructured social media data and extracting clinically relevant insights.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper claims to be a comprehensive survey, differentiating itself from existing literature by covering a broader range of mental health disorders and by focusing specifically on the application of LLMs in social media analysis. It also highlights the unique characteristics of social media data (interactivity, dynamics, real-time nature) which are often overlooked in general mental health-focused LLM surveys. The claim is partially justified. While surveys exist on LLMs in mental health, and others on mental health detection using social media, the explicit synthesis of the *two* with a wider disease scope (beyond depression and anxiety) provides some incremental novelty.  The added value lies in the paper's consolidation of information in one place. However, the individual components are not necessarily novel. Many papers are focusing on single diseases and do not have the generalizability of other disorders such as schizophrenia.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to provide a valuable resource for researchers entering this rapidly evolving field. By consolidating information on LLM applications, available datasets, and challenges, it can help accelerate research and development efforts. Furthermore, by identifying gaps in current research, it can guide future investigations and contribute to the advancement of mental health detection and intervention strategies. The work on datasets provides another framework for applying Large Language Models to data. However, the significance is somewhat limited by the fact that LLM research progresses very rapidly, meaning a survey like this may become dated relatively quickly. The potential to speed up research is significant, but the specific examples might already be overcome.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The survey addresses a wide spectrum of mental health disorders and various facets of LLM application.
    *   **Practical Focus:** It highlights specific applications, datasets, and evaluation metrics, making it directly useful for researchers.
    *   **Identifies Research Gaps:** The paper clearly outlines areas where future research is needed.
    *   **Good Structure:** The organization (Introduction, Preliminary, LLMs Applications, Datasets, Conclusion) is logical and supports clear communication of ideas.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Rapid Obsolescence:**  The field of LLM is developing quickly. Some information may be out of date quickly.
    *   **Limited Depth:**  Given the breadth of the topic, some aspects are covered at a relatively high level, potentially lacking in-depth analysis. The lack of depth is also exacerbated by the fact that mental health is a sensitive topic, leading to possible errors.
    *   **Descriptive vs. Analytical:**  While the paper presents a good overview, there's a degree of description rather than a deeper analytical synthesis and critical comparison of different approaches.
    *   **Overstated Claims:** The survey frames itself with statements such as providing a "comprehensive frame of reference" and demonstrating the "great potential of LLMs", but such claims should be carefully evaluated given the limitations in current research and the rapid advancement of the technology.
    *   **Some citations may not be recent, which limits generalizability. **

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to serve as a valuable introductory resource, particularly for researchers new to the intersection of LLMs and mental health. However, its long-term influence will depend on how well it stays updated with the latest advances in the field and how it can stimulate novel research directions. Given the current state of the field, many of the papers that would be included will focus more on datasets rather than the models to apply. The dataset can be improved to generalize across different categories of text.

**Score:** 6.5

**Justification:**

The paper is a useful, organized overview that consolidates relevant information about LLMs for mental health detection on social media. It offers some incremental novelty by encompassing a wider range of mental health disorders and by highlighting social media-specific nuances. Its significance lies in its potential to accelerate research by providing a quick reference guide. However, the limitations in depth, the descriptive nature of the analysis, and, most importantly, the risk of rapid obsolescence prevent it from being a truly exceptional and transformative contribution. A score of 6.5 reflects its value as a starting point and its potential, tempered by the challenges it faces in maintaining relevance and impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CL

### F-ViTA: Foundation Model Guided Visible to Thermal Translation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02801v1)
- **Authors**: Jay N. Paranjape, Celso de Melo, Vishal M. Patel
- **Abstract**: Thermal imaging is crucial for scene understanding, particularly in low-light and nighttime conditions. However, collecting large thermal datasets is costly and labor-intensive due to the specialized equipment required for infrared image capture. To address this challenge, researchers have explored visible-to-thermal image translation. Most existing methods rely on Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) or Diffusion Models (DMs), treating the task as a style transfer problem. As a result, these approaches attempt to learn both the modality distribution shift and underlying physical principles from limited training data. In this paper, we propose F-ViTA, a novel approach that leverages the general world knowledge embedded in foundation models to guide the diffusion process for improved translation. Specifically, we condition an InstructPix2Pix Diffusion Model with zero-shot masks and labels from foundation models such as SAM and Grounded DINO. This allows the model to learn meaningful correlations between scene objects and their thermal signatures in infrared imagery. Extensive experiments on five public datasets demonstrate that F-ViTA outperforms state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods. Furthermore, our model generalizes well to out-of-distribution (OOD) scenarios and can generate Long-Wave Infrared (LWIR), Mid-Wave Infrared (MWIR), and Near-Infrared (NIR) translations from the same visible image. Code: https://github.com/JayParanjape/F-ViTA/tree/master.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "F-VITA: Foundation Model Guided Visible to Thermal Translation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces F-ViTA, a novel approach for visible-to-thermal image translation. It leverages pre-trained foundation models (FMs) like SAM and Grounded DINO to guide a diffusion model. Specifically, it extracts object tags and segmentation masks from visible images in a zero-shot manner, using these as conditioning information during the diffusion process.  This helps the model learn correlations between scene objects and their thermal signatures.  The paper demonstrates that F-ViTA outperforms state-of-the-art methods on various public datasets and can generalize to out-of-distribution scenarios, even generating different infrared bands (LWIR, MWIR, NIR) based on text prompts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the application of foundation models (specifically, their zero-shot capabilities for object detection and segmentation) to guide the diffusion process for visible-to-thermal translation. While using GANs or Diffusion Models for this task isn't new, leveraging FMs to provide semantic and localization priors represents a genuine advance. The ability to generate different IR bands with text prompts is another novel contribution. A more standard approach has been to train with pairs of images.

**Significance:** Thermal imaging is important for autonomous driving, surveillance, and other applications, but collecting large, paired datasets is costly. An effective visible-to-thermal translation method can mitigate this data scarcity. F-ViTA's improved performance and generalization compared to existing methods, coupled with its text-guided capabilities, make it a significant contribution. This has the potential to improve the performance of various downstream vision tasks. The out-of-distribution (OOD) results and segmentation benchmarks further underscore the practical value of the research.

**Strengths:**

*   **Effective Use of FMs:** The paper demonstrates a well-integrated approach using FMs for object detection, segmentation, and label extraction. The design of the conditioning mechanism within the diffusion model is reasonable and seems to work well.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper provides comprehensive experimental results on five datasets and multiple metrics (FID, LPIPS, SSIM, PSNR), consistently outperforming existing SOTA. The ablation studies offer insights into the importance of different FM components.
*   **Out-of-Distribution Generalization:**  The OOD experiments on MFNet are valuable and demonstrate a clear advantage of the F-ViTA approach.
*   **Text-Prompted Translation:** The ability to generate LWIR, MWIR, and NIR images from a single visible image with text guidance is a significant innovation.
*   **Downstream Application:** The downstream application experiments in segmentation and detection provide the evidence of usability.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **FID score on FLIR:** While generally the results are superior to current methods, the fid score is lower in on test.
*   **Clarity on the FM Integration:** While the paper explains that foundation models help to localize and semantically understand the visual data, it doesn't provide an in-depth theoretical justification for the observed improvements. It would be helpful to have a more formal analysis of why the FMs lead to better physical representation of heat.
*   **Dependence on FM Performance:** The performance of F-ViTA is intrinsically tied to the quality of the foundation models used (RAM, Grounded DINO, SAM). While the paper uses strong FMs, improvements or limitations in those models will directly impact F-ViTA. This reliance should be discussed more explicitly. The paper does well in stating in what instances the method can have failures.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a significant advancement in visible-to-thermal translation by cleverly incorporating foundation models into a diffusion model framework. The strong experimental results, including OOD generalization and text-prompted translation capabilities, are compelling. However, there are some limitations, particularly in explaining how the FM embeddings influence the generation. The method depends on high performance foundation models, which can become updated over time and have a performance effect on the result.

### MegaMath: Pushing the Limits of Open Math Corpora
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02807v1)
- **Authors**: Fan Zhou, Zengzhi Wang, Nikhil Ranjan, Zhoujun Cheng, Liping Tang, Guowei He, Zhengzhong Liu, Eric P. Xing
- **Abstract**: Mathematical reasoning is a cornerstone of human intelligence and a key benchmark for advanced capabilities in large language models (LLMs). However, the research community still lacks an open, large-scale, high-quality corpus tailored to the demands of math-centric LLM pre-training. We present MegaMath, an open dataset curated from diverse, math-focused sources through following practices: (1) Revisiting web data: We re-extracted mathematical documents from Common Crawl with math-oriented HTML optimizations, fasttext-based filtering and deduplication, all for acquiring higher-quality data on the Internet. (2) Recalling Math-related code data: We identified high quality math-related code from large code training corpus, Stack-V2, further enhancing data diversity. (3) Exploring Synthetic data: We synthesized QA-style text, math-related code, and interleaved text-code blocks from web data or code data. By integrating these strategies and validating their effectiveness through extensive ablations, MegaMath delivers 371B tokens with the largest quantity and top quality among existing open math pre-training datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the MegaMath paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MegaMath, a new open-source dataset for math-centric large language model (LLM) pre-training.  The dataset is curated from diverse sources using several novel approaches: (1) re-extraction and filtering of Common Crawl data with math-specific optimizations; (2)  incorporation of math-related code from the Stack V2 corpus; and (3) generation of synthetic QA data and interleaved text-code blocks.  The resulting MegaMath dataset comprises 371B tokens, which the authors claim is the largest and of higher quality than existing open math pre-training datasets. The paper details the data curation pipelines, ablation studies validating key design choices, and empirical demonstrations of the dataset's effectiveness through pre-training experiments. The authors also demonstrate pre-training on the latest Llama-3 series of models, showing improvements on math-related benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Scale and Openness:**  The most significant strength is the size and open availability of the MegaMath dataset. Providing a large, high-quality, math-focused corpus to the research community addresses a crucial need.
*   **Novel Data Curation Strategies:**  The paper introduces several novel data curation techniques that are specifically tailored for mathematical content. The re-extraction and optimized filtering of Common Crawl data, the targeted inclusion of math-related code, and the generation of synthetic data are valuable contributions. These methods are well-documented and justified.
*   **Comprehensive Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies are a significant strength, rigorously evaluating the impact of various design choices within the data curation pipelines.  These experiments provide valuable insights into the effectiveness of each component and inform future research.
*   **Empirical Validation:**  The paper includes empirical demonstrations of MegaMath's effectiveness through pre-training experiments, showing improvements on standard math benchmarks when using Llama models trained on this dataset.
* **Strong reproducibility** The authors provided enough details regarding all the steps for constructing the dataset.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty of Individual Techniques:** While the overall approach is innovative, many of the individual techniques (e.g., using fastText for filtering, LLMs for refining) are building upon existing methods. The *combination* and *application* within the math domain is where the novelty lies.
*   **Reliance on LLMs for Data Refinement:**  The use of LLMs for data refinement in MegaMath-Web-Pro and the synthesis of data introduces a potential for bias.  While the authors acknowledge this, a more in-depth analysis of potential biases and their impact on downstream performance would strengthen the paper. This approach can be expensive because it requires lots of compute power and costs for LLM's API.
*   **Benchmark Contamination:** Despite the authors' attempts to decontaminate the dataset, the potential for benchmark contamination remains a concern.  The authors only checked for exact 13-gram matches, while more sophisticated methods (e.g. fuzzy matching) would be desirable.

**Significance:**

MegaMath has the potential to significantly impact the field of math-focused LLMs.  By providing a large, open, and high-quality dataset, it can accelerate research in this area and enable the development of more capable math reasoning models. The detailed data curation pipelines and ablation studies provide valuable guidance for future dataset construction efforts. However, it is essential to acknowledge the potential for bias introduced by the use of LLMs in the data curation process and the ongoing challenge of benchmark contamination.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 7.5/10.  The dataset's size, open access, and the comprehensive approach to data curation represent significant contributions. The detailed ablation studies are also highly valuable. However, the incremental nature of some individual techniques and the potential for LLM-introduced bias and benchmark contamination prevents it from being a higher-scoring breakthrough.

### Generative Evaluation of Complex Reasoning in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02810v1)
- **Authors**: Haowei Lin, Xiangyu Wang, Ruilin Yan, Baizhou Huang, Haotian Ye, Jianhua Zhu, Zihao Wang, James Zou, Jianzhu Ma, Yitao Liang
- **Abstract**: With powerful large language models (LLMs) demonstrating superhuman reasoning capabilities, a critical question arises: Do LLMs genuinely reason, or do they merely recall answers from their extensive, web-scraped training datasets? Publicly released benchmarks inevitably become contaminated once incorporated into subsequent LLM training sets, undermining their reliability as faithful assessments. To address this, we introduce KUMO, a generative evaluation framework designed specifically for assessing reasoning in LLMs. KUMO synergistically combines LLMs with symbolic engines to dynamically produce diverse, multi-turn reasoning tasks that are partially observable and adjustable in difficulty. Through an automated pipeline, KUMO continuously generates novel tasks across open-ended domains, compelling models to demonstrate genuine generalization rather than memorization. We evaluated 23 state-of-the-art LLMs on 5,000 tasks across 100 domains created by KUMO, benchmarking their reasoning abilities against university students. Our findings reveal that many LLMs have outperformed university-level performance on easy reasoning tasks, and reasoning-scaled LLMs reach university-level performance on complex reasoning challenges. Moreover, LLM performance on KUMO tasks correlates strongly with results on newly released real-world reasoning benchmarks, underscoring KUMO's value as a robust, enduring assessment tool for genuine LLM reasoning capabilities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Generative evaluation of complex reasoning in large language models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces KUMO, a generative evaluation framework designed to assess complex reasoning abilities in Large Language Models (LLMs). KUMO addresses the issue of data contamination in existing benchmarks by dynamically generating diverse, multi-turn reasoning tasks across various open-ended domains using LLMs and symbolic engines.  The framework allows for adjustable difficulty and partial observability, compelling models to generalize rather than memorize. The authors evaluate 23 state-of-the-art LLMs on 5,000 KUMO-generated tasks across 100 domains, benchmarking their performance against university students. The results show that some LLMs outperform university-level performance on easier tasks and achieve comparable performance on complex ones.  Furthermore, LLM performance on KUMO strongly correlates with newly released real-world reasoning benchmarks, validating its efficacy as a robust assessment tool. The paper also explores the framework's resistance to overfitting and analyzes how domain graph topology influences reasoning performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of dynamically generating reasoning tasks to avoid data contamination is a significant contribution.  While generative approaches exist in the context of evaluating logical reasoning and query processing (LogicBench, DYVAL), KUMO's advancement lies in its ability to create rich, contextualized, multi-turn reasoning games. The incorporation of symbolic engines for task generation combined with LLMs for content creation provides a good balance between control and diversity. Furthermore, the study of domain graph topology and its correlation with LLM reasoning performance is an interesting aspect.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem in LLM evaluation: the degradation of benchmark reliability due to data contamination. KUMO offers a promising solution that can provide more trustworthy and enduring assessments of LLM reasoning capabilities. The strong correlation with real-world benchmarks further reinforces the significance of this work. The framework’s scalability and adaptability also make it potentially valuable to the community.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-designed and comprehensive framework.
    *   Extensive experimental validation with a large number of models and tasks.
    *   Detailed analysis of the results, including overfitting resistance and domain influence.
    *   Robustness to data contamination shown through experimentation.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Reliance on LLMs for certain components (domain proposal, knowledge book generation) can introduce bias or limitations based on the capabilities of the underlying LLMs.
    *   While correlations with real-world benchmarks are shown, a more direct demonstration of how KUMO can differentiate between models that genuinely reason and those that memorize would strengthen the claims further.
    *   The comparison to human performance, while present, could be more rigorously analyzed. The experiment is small, so it is hard to make strong claims around human performance based off that.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of LLM evaluation.  The dynamic task generation approach effectively tackles the problem of data contamination, and the correlation with real-world benchmarks validates the framework's relevance. While there are some limitations associated with the reliance on LLMs within the generation pipeline and the need for more evidence that KUMO can differentiate between memorization and real reasoning, the overall impact of this work is substantial. The framework provides a more reliable and scalable assessment tool, which can be valuable for guiding future LLM development.
